D_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{D}^{\vee})
is given by the matrix
w_{0}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1})^{T}w_{0}
. Let
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1}=(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{ij})
, then
w_{0}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1})^{T}w_{0}=(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{n+1-j,n+1-i})_{i<j}
. In particular,
\{\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}\}_{1\leq t\leq l}
encodes the information of
\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{n-q,j}/\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{n-q,n-l}\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j<n-l\\
n-l+1\leq q\leq n\end{subarray}}
.

Write

\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D})\\
0&B\end{array}\right),

where
A
(resp.,
B
) is a matrix of size
l\times l
(resp.,
(n-l)\times(n-l)
). Therefore,

\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{-1}&-A^{-1}\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D})B^{-1}\\
0&B^{-1}\end{array}\right).

Therefore,
\{\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}\}_{1\leq t\leq l}
gives the matrix
\diag\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1,n-l},\cdots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{l,n-l}\}^{-1}A^{-1}\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D})B^{-1}
, and
\{\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner}_{q}\}_{l+1\leq q\leq n}
gives the matrix
\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{D})\diag\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{l,l+1},\cdots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{l,n}\}^{-1}
. The result follows.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

Assume that
q>r+1
. Suppose that
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{q^{\prime}}_{r^{\prime}}
is already determined for all
r\leq r^{\prime}<q^{\prime}\leq q
with
(r^{\prime},q^{\prime})\neq(r,q)
. Then
\mathcal{C}_{r}^{q}
together with the lower-left entry
\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q-1}+1}
of
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{q}_{r}
determines
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{q}_{r}
(equivalently,
\mathcal{C}_{r}^{q}
determines
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{q}_{r}
up to one entry).

Proof.

For each
1\leq t\leq l_{q}
, recall that
E_{q}^{(t)}\subseteq E_{q}
is the unique
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of rank
t
. Then

\mathbf{D}=[(E_{q}^{(t)})^{\prime}-(E_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime}-E_{q}/E_{q}^{(t)}].

By quotienting the basis of
D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{q}^{(t)})^{\prime})
in (
3.2
), we obtain an induced Hodge filtration on
D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime})
, and hence on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{r}^{\prime})
. Note that
\rk E_{r}^{q-1}=t_{r}^{q-1}
. Consider the
t_{r}^{q-1}
-order minor
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[t_{r-1}+1+t,t_{q-1}+t]}_{[t_{r-1}+1,t_{q-1}]}
. Using elementary column operations, we obtain an upper triangular matrix
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner}_{t}
with all diagonal entries equal to
1
, supported on the
(t_{r-1}+1)
-th to
t_{r}
-th rows and the
(t_{r-1}+t+1)
-th to
(t_{r}+t)
-th columns. This determines the complete flag
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{r}^{\prime})
on
E_{r}^{\prime}
.

On the other hand, for each
1\leq t\leq l_{r}
, consider the quotient map
E_{r}\twoheadrightarrow E_{r}^{(t)}
of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. Then

\mathbf{D}=[\ker(E_{r}\twoheadrightarrow E_{r}^{(t)})-(E_{r+1}^{q})^{\prime}-(E_{r}^{(t)})^{\prime}].

By quotienting the basis of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\ker(E_{r}\twoheadrightarrow E_{r}^{(t)}))
in (
3.2
), we obtain an induced Hodge filtration
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{r+1}^{q})^{\prime})
on
D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{r+1}^{q})^{\prime})
. Consider the
t_{r}^{q}\times(t_{r}^{q}-t)
-order minor
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[t_{r-1}+1,t_{q}]}_{[t_{r-1}+t+1,t_{q}]}
. Using the
[t_{q}-(l_{r}-t)+1,t_{q}]
-columns of
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[t_{r-1}+1,t_{q}]}_{[t_{r-1}+t+1,t_{q}]}
and elementary column operations, we transform
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[t_{q}-(l_{r}-t)+1,t_{q}]}_{[t_{r-1}+t,t_{r}]}
(resp.,
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[t_{r-1}+1,t_{q}-(l_{r}-t)]}_{[t_{r-1}+t,t_{q}]}
) into an upper triangular matrix
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}
with unit diagonal entries (resp., the zero matrix). Hence the induced Hodge filtration on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{q}^{\prime})
is encoded by
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}
.

It remains to check that
\{\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner}_{t}\}_{1\leq t\leq l_{q}}
and

\{\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}\}_{1\leq t\leq l_{r}}
encode enough information. Let
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\pm 1}^{(r,q)}
be the space of rational functions in the matrix entries of
\{\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})_{r^{\prime}}^{q^{\prime}}\}_{r\leq r^{\prime}<q^{\prime}\leq q,(r^{\prime},q^{\prime})\neq(r,q)}
with coefficients in
\{\pm 1\}
. Then, for
1\leq t\leq l_{q}
, a careful inspection of the elementary column operations shows that the last column of
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner}_{t}
equals
(H_{1,t}/H_{l_{r},t},\cdots,H_{l_{r},t}/H_{l_{r},t})^{T}
, where

H_{j,t}:=H_{j,t}\big(\{\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+j,t_{q-1}+l}\}_{1\leq l\leq t}\big)

are certain linear forms in the variables
\{\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+j,t_{q-1}+l}\}_{1\leq l\leq t}
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\pm 1}^{(r,q)}
, for each
1\leq j<l_{r}
. In particular, if we regard
\{\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q-1}+t}\}_{1\leq t\leq l_{q}}
as free variables, then each
\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+j,t_{q-1}+t}
can be expressed as a linear combination of these free variables with known coefficients.

By the duality relation between
\iota^{r,q,+}_{\mathbf{D}}(F_{t})
and
\iota^{r,q,-}_{\mathbf{D}^{\vee}}(F_{t}^{\vee})
, we obtain an explicit description of
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}
. With respect to the dual basis of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{D}^{\vee})\cong D_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{D})^{\vee}
, the Hodge filtration on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{D}^{\vee})
is given by the matrix
w_{0}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1})^{T}w_{0}
, i.e., if
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1}=(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{ij})
, then
w_{0}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1})^{T}w_{0}=(\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{ij})_{i<j}
with
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{ij}:=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{n+1-j,n+1-i}
. If we write

\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{D})\\
0&B\end{array}\right),

where
A
(resp.,
B
) is a matrix of order
t_{r}^{q-1}\times t_{r}^{q-1}
(resp.,
l_{q}\times l_{q}
). Then we see that

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{-1}&-A^{-1}\underline{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{D})B^{-1}\\
0&B^{-1}\end{array}\right).

(3.13)

Thus the last column of
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{t}
equals
(H^{\prime}_{1,t}/H^{\prime}_{l_{q},t},\cdots,H^{\prime}_{l_{q},t}/H^{\prime}_{l_{q},t})^{T}
, where

H^{\prime}_{j,t}:=H^{\prime}_{j,t}\big(\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{t_{q+1}^{s}+j,t^{s}_{r-1}+l}\}_{1\leq l\leq l_{r}-t+1}\big)=H^{\prime}_{j,t}\big(\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{t_{r}+1-l,t_{q}+1-j}\}_{1\leq l\leq l_{r}-t+1}\big)

are certain linear forms in the variables
\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{t_{r}+1-l,t_{q}+1-j}\}_{1\leq l\leq l_{r}-t}
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\pm 1}^{(r,q)}
(using (
3.13
)), for each
1\leq l\leq l_{r}
. Furthermore, using (
3.13
) again, we see that

H^{\prime}_{j,t}\big(\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{t_{r}+1-l,t_{q}+1-j}\}_{1\leq l\leq l_{r}-t+1}\big)=H^{\prime\prime}_{j,t}\big(\{\mathcal{L}_{t_{r}+1-l,t_{q}+1-j}\}_{1\leq l\leq l_{r}-t+1}\big)

for another family of linear forms
H^{\prime\prime}_{j,t}
in the variables
\{\mathcal{L}_{t_{r}+1-l,t_{q}+1-j}\}_{1\leq l\leq l_{r}-t}
with coefficients in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\pm 1}^{(r,q)}
. In particular, taking
t=l_{r}
, the collection

\Big\{\frac{H^{\prime\prime}_{j,t}(\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q}+1-j})}{H^{\prime\prime}_{l_{r},q}(\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q}+1-l_{q}})}\Big\}_{1\leq j<l_{q}}

is captured by
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{l_{r}}
, so we can express
\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q}+1-j}
in terms of
\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q}+1-l_{q}}
and
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\llcorner}_{l_{r}}
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+j,t_{q-1}+t}
is a linear combination of
\{\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q-1}+t}\}_{1\leq t\leq l_{q}}
with known coefficients, we obtain

\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+j,t_{q-1}+t}=A_{j,t}\mathcal{L}_{t_{r-1}+l_{r},t_{q}+1-l_{q}}+B_{j,t},

where
A_{j,t}
and
B_{j,t}
are known coefficients. This completes the proof.
∎

3.2
“Crystalline” Hodge parameters inside
\mathbf{D}

This section follows the strategy in
[
15
, Section 2.2-2.4]
and develops a block version (see also
[
22
, Section 3.1]
for the potentially crystalline case). Our goal is to extract the Hodge parameters of
\mathbf{D}
that arise from the “crystalline” Hodge parameters between Steinberg blocks and crystalline subquotients of
\mathbf{D}
.

Let
1\leq r<q\leq s
. Consider
E_{r}^{q}=[E_{r}^{q-1}-E_{q}]=[E_{q}^{\prime}-(E_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime}]
. Applying the discussion around (
3.5
) and (
3.7
) to
E^{q}_{r}
and
H=E^{r+1}_{r}\hookrightarrow E^{q-1}_{r}
, we obtain a map

\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}}:=\iota^{-}_{E_{r}^{q}}(E_{q},E^{r+1}_{r}):E_{r}^{r+1}\rightarrow(E_{r}^{r+1})^{\prime}.

Note that if
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{r}^{r+1},(E_{r}^{r+1})^{\prime})=2
(hence
q>r+1
), then
\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}}
encodes one additional parameter.

On the other hand, any maximal crystalline subquotient
\mathbf{D}_{0}
of
\mathbf{D}
has the form:

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{0}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{1}p^{j_{1}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{2}p^{j_{2}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+2}})-\cdots-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{s}p^{j_{s}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s}})].

(3.14)

for integers
0\leq j_{i}\leq l_{i}-1
(
1\leq i\leq s
) and some
0\leq b\leq n-s
. In particular,
\mathbf{D}
admits a unique (maximal) crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule (resp., quotient)
\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cr}}
(resp.,
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}
). More precisely,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{cr}}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{2})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{2}})-\cdots-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{s})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{s}})],

(3.15)

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{1}p^{l_{1}-1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n-s+1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{2}p^{l_{2}-1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n-s+2}})-\cdots-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{s}p^{l_{s}-1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n}})].\;

Remark 3.6
.

We explain why we consider crystalline subquotients. First, the Hodge parameters in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ST}}
and the “crystalline” Hodge parameters are tightly intertwined, and crystalline subquotients provide a way to isolate the purely crystalline part. Second, the Hodge parameters of crystalline subquotients can be captured by explicit locally analytic representations, which suggests an internal structure for the conjectural locally analytic representation
\pi(\mathbf{D})
.

We apply the above discussion to a crystalline subquotient
\mathbf{D}_{0}
of
\mathbf{D}
of the form (
3.14
) (i.e.,
\rk(E_{i})=1
for all
1\leq i\leq s
). The moduli space of non-critical crystalline
p
-adic Hodge parameters is:

\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}^{\mathrm{{cr}}}:=\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}(\emptyset)=\bigcap_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{\mathbf{T}}_{s}\backslash u\mathbf{B}_{s}w_{0,s}\mathbf{B}_{s}/\mathbf{B}_{s}\subseteq{\mathbf{T}}_{s}\backslash\mathbf{B}_{s}w_{0,s}\mathbf{B}_{s}/\mathbf{B}_{s}.

For
1\leq r<q\leq s
with
q>r+1
, consider the following refinement of
\mathbf{D}_{0}
:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{q}=[(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{q-1}-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{q}p^{j_{q}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+q}})]=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{q}p^{j_{q}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+r}})-((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime}]

(3.16)

Then the composition
(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}(\psi_{r}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+r}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}(\psi_{r+1}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+r+1}})]\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{q}\twoheadrightarrow((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime}
factors through
((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1})^{\prime}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}(\psi_{r}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+r+1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}(\psi_{r+1}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+r+2}})]\hookrightarrow((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime}
. Hence we obtain a natural map

\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}_{0}}:(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1}\rightarrow(\mathbf{D}_{0})^{r+1}_{r,[q]}:=((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1})^{\prime}.

By
[
15
, Remark 2.3]
, we have

Lemma 3.7
.

We have
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1},((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1})^{\prime})=2
.

Theorem 3.8
.

The Hodge parameters of
\mathbf{D}_{0}
are determined by the data
\{\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}_{0}}\}_{1\leq r<r+1<q\leq s}
.

Proof.

The case
s=2
is clear. Assume the theorem holds for every crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
s-1
. We prove it for rank
s
. Note that
\mathbf{D}_{0}=[(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{1}^{s-1}-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{s}p^{j_{s}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s}})]=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha_{1}p^{j_{1}})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+1}})-(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{2}^{s}]
. By the induction hypothesis, the
p
-adic Hodge parameters of
(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{1}^{s-1}
are determined by
\{\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}_{0}}\}_{1\leq r<r+1<q\leq s-1}
. Hence it remains to determine the line
e_{b+s}+\mathcal{L}_{b+s-1,b+s}e_{b+s-1}+\cdots+\mathcal{L}_{b+1,b+s}e_{b+1}
in
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s}}^{H}D_{\mathrm{cr}}(\mathbf{D}_{0})
. Applying the induction hypothesis to
(\mathbf{D}_{0})_{2}^{s}
, we know that
\{\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}_{0}}\}_{2\leq r<r+1<q\leq s}
determines the vector
e_{b+s}+\mathcal{L}_{b+s-1,b+s}e_{b+s-1}+\cdots+\mathcal{L}_{b+2,b+s}e_{b+2}
. Therefore it suffices to determine the remaining parameter
\mathcal{L}_{b+1,b+s}
. Write
(D_{0})_{1}^{s-1}:=D_{\mathrm{cr}}(((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})
. The induced Hodge filtration on
(D_{0})_{1}^{s-1}
is:

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{j}_{H}((D_{0})_{1}^{s-1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(D_{0})_{1}^{s-1},&j\in(-\infty,-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+1}],\\
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+3}}^{H}((D_{0})_{1}^{s-1})\oplus E(e_{b+2}+\mathcal{L}_{b+1,b+2}e_{b+1}),&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+1},-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+2}],\\
\cdots&\cdots\\
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s}}^{H}((D_{0})_{1}^{s-1})\oplus E(e_{b+s-1}+\sum_{l=1}^{s-2}\mathcal{L}_{b+l,b+s-1}e_{b+l}),&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s-2},-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s-1}]\\
E(e_{b+s-1}+\sum_{l=1}^{s-2}\mathcal{L}_{b+l,b+s}e_{b+l}),&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s-1},-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s}]\\
0,&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s},+\infty).\end{array}\right.

(3.17)

Using
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s}}^{H}((D_{0})_{1}^{s-1})
, we can translate the line
E(e_{b+s-1}+\sum_{l=1}^{s-2}\mathcal{L}_{b+l,b+s-1}e_{b+l})
in
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s-1}}^{H}((D_{0})_{1}^{s-1})
into the
E
-line

E\Big(e_{b+s-2}+\sum_{l=1}^{s-3}\frac{\mathcal{L}_{b+l,b+s-1}-\mathcal{L}_{b+l,b+s}}{\mathcal{L}_{b+s-2,b+s-1}-\mathcal{L}_{b+s-2,b+s}}e_{b+l}\Big).

Using this new
E
-line, we can translate the line
E(e_{b+s-2}+\sum_{l=1}^{s-3}\mathcal{L}_{b+l,b+s-2}e_{b+l})
in
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{h}}_{b+s-1}}^{H}((D_{0})_{1}^{s-1})
into an
E
-line
E(e_{b+s-3}+\sum_{l=1}^{s-3}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b+l,b+s-3}e_{b+l})
. Note that
\mathcal{L}_{b+1,b+s}
appears in these coefficients
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b+l,b+s-3}
. Iterating this procedure, we eventually obtain an
E
-line
E(e_{b+2}+\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b+1,b+2}e_{b+1})
in which
\mathcal{L}_{b+1,b+s}
appears in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b+1,b+2}
. Since
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b+1,b+2}
is captured by the morphism
\iota^{1,s}_{\mathbf{D}_{0}}
, the parameter
\mathcal{L}_{b+1,b+s}
is determined by
\iota^{1,s}_{\mathbf{D}_{0}}
. This completes the induction step.
∎

Theorem
3.8
is equivalent to the following statement.

Theorem 3.9
.

The following morphism is injective:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}^{\mathrm{{cr}}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\prod_{1\leq r<r+1<q\leq s}{\mathbf{T}}_{2}\backslash\left({\rm GL}_{2}/\mathbf{B}_{2}\times{\rm GL}_{2}/\mathbf{B}_{2}\right),

(3.18)

\displaystyle[\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D}_{0})]

\displaystyle\mapsto[({\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{\mathrm{cris}}((\mathbf{D}_{0})_{r}^{r+1}),{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{\mathrm{cris}}((\mathbf{D}_{0})^{r+1}_{r,[q]}))]

where
{\rm GL}_{2}/\mathbf{B}_{2}\times{\rm GL}_{2}/\mathbf{B}_{2}
carries the diagonal action of
{\mathbf{T}}_{2}
.

3.3
Capture all the Hodge parameters via different refinements

In this section, we prove the following theorems.

Theorem 3.10
.

\mathbf{D}
is uniquely determined by the data
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ST}}
and
\{\iota^{r^{\prime},q^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}}\}_{1\leq r^{\prime}<q^{\prime}\leq s}
(equivalently,
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}
).

Theorem 3.11
.

\mathbf{D}
is uniquely determined by the data
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ST}}
and
\{\iota^{r,q}_{\mathbf{D}}\}_{1\leq r\leq r+1<q\leq s}
.

Proof of Theorem
3.10
.

We prove Theorem
3.10
by induction on
s
. There is nothing to prove for
s=1
, and the case
s=2
is Theorem
3.4
. Assume the statement holds for every semistable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
n-1
, and prove it for rank
n
. Write

\mathbf{D}=[\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1}-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{n})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n}})]\qquad(\text{resp., }\mathbf{D}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1}})-\mathbf{D}_{2}^{n}]).

In particular, there is a surjection
E_{s}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{n})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n}})
(resp., an injection
\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{1})z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1}})\hookrightarrow E_{1}
) of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. By the induction hypothesis,
\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1}
(resp.,
\mathbf{D}_{2}^{n}
) is determined by the corresponding data. Using
\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1}
, it remains to determine the line

{\rm Fil}^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n}}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbf{D})=E(e_{n}+\mathcal{L}_{n-1,n}e_{n-1}+\cdots+\mathcal{L}_{1,n}e_{1}).

On the other hand, the vector
e_{n}+\mathcal{L}_{n-1,n}e_{n-1}+\cdots+\mathcal{L}_{2,n}e_{2}
is already captured by
D_{2}^{n}
, so only the parameter
\mathcal{L}_{1,n}
remains. If
\mathbf{D}
is crystalline, this is exactly Theorem
3.8
. Hence we may assume that
\mathbf{D}
is semistable non-crystalline and that
l_{1}>1
. Write
\mathbf{D}=[(E_{2}^{s})^{\prime}-E_{1}^{\prime}]
and consider the following induced Hodge filtration on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{\prime})
(obtained by quotienting the basis
e_{l_{1}+1},\cdots,e_{n}
of
D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{2}^{s})^{\prime})
in (
3.2
)):

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{j}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{\prime}),&j\in(-\infty,-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-l_{1}+1}],\\
{\rm Fil}^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n-l_{1}+3}}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{\prime})\oplus E(\sum_{l=1}^{l_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{l,n-l_{1}+2}e_{l}),&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-l_{1}+1},-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-l_{1}+2}],\\
\cdots&\cdots\\
{\rm Fil}^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n}}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{\prime})\oplus E(\sum_{l=1}^{l_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{l,n-1}e_{l}),&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-2},-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-1}]\\
E(\sum_{l=1}^{l_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{l,n}e_{l}),&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-1},-{\mathbf{h}}_{n}]\\
0,&j\in(-{\mathbf{h}}_{n},+\infty).\end{array}\right.

(3.19)

In particular, the line
E(\sum_{l=1}^{l_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{l,n}e_{l})
is captured by
E_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ST}}
. Since
\{\mathcal{L}_{l,n}\}_{2\leq l\leq l_{1}}
is already determined by the induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{L}_{1,n}
is also determined. This completes the induction step.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.11
.

We prove the theorem by induction on
s
. The statement is trivial for
s=1
, and the case
s=2
is Theorem
3.4
. Assume it holds for
s-1
. Applying the induction hypothesis to
E_{1}^{s-1}
and
E_{2}^{s}
, it remains to determine
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})_{1}^{s}
. By Proposition
3.5
, it is enough to determine the lower-left entry
\mathcal{L}_{l_{1},t_{s-1}+1}
of
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{s}_{1}
. We show that
\mathcal{L}_{l_{1},t_{s-1}+1}
is determined by
\iota^{1,s}_{\mathbf{D}}
. Write

\mathbf{D}=[E_{1}^{s-1}-E_{s}]=[E_{s}^{\prime}-(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}].

By quotienting the basis of
D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{q})
in (
3.2
), we obtain an induced Hodge filtration
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime})
on
D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{r}^{q-1})^{\prime})
. Consider the
t_{s-1}
-order minor
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[1+l_{s},n]}_{[1,t_{s-1}]}
. Using elementary column operations, we transform
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{[1+l_{s},n]}_{[1,t_{s-1}]}
into an upper triangular matrix
\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner}
with unit diagonal entries. Then
(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner})^{[l_{s}+1,l_{s}+l_{1}+l_{2}]}_{[1,l_{1}+l_{2}]}
determines the complete flag
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{\bullet}D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{1}^{2})^{\prime})
. Hence the map
\iota^{1,s}_{\mathbf{D}}:E_{1}^{2}\rightarrow(E_{1}^{2})^{\prime}
induces an injection of filtered
(\varphi,N)
-modules

\iota^{1,s}_{\mathbf{D}}:{\rm Fil}_{H}^{\bullet}D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{2})\rightarrow{\rm Fil}_{H}^{\bullet}D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{1}^{2})^{\prime}),

whose underlying map of
(\varphi,N)
-modules is the identity on
D_{\mathrm{st}}(E_{1}^{2})\rightarrow D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{1}^{2})^{\prime})
. In particular,
\iota^{1,s}_{\mathbf{D}}
compares the two matrices
(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D}))^{[1,l_{1}+l_{2}]}_{[1,l_{1}+l_{2}]}
and
(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})^{\urcorner})^{[l_{s}+1,l_{s}+l_{1}+l_{2}]}_{[1,l_{1}+l_{2}]}
. This comparison determines
\mathcal{L}_{l_{1},t_{s-1}+1}
, since
\mathcal{L}_{l_{1},t_{s-1}+1}
appears in
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{{\mathbf{h}}_{l_{s}+l_{1}}}D_{\mathrm{st}}((E_{1}^{2})^{\prime})
by tracing the elementary column operations.
∎

Let
u_{0}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
be an element satisfying
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}=[R_{u_{0},n-s+1}-\cdots-R_{u_{0},n}]
. Note that
{\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{n-s-1}}\cong{\rm GL}_{n-s}\times{\rm GL}_{s}
. Let
{\mathbf{T}}_{s}\subseteq\mathbf{B}_{s}\subseteq{\rm GL}_{s}
be the corresponding torus and standard Borel subgroup. We have the following natural maps:

p_{\widehat{n-s-1}}:\mathbf{B}w_{0}\mathbf{B}\cong\mathbf{N}_{\emptyset}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{N}_{\emptyset}/\mathbf{N}_{\widehat{n-s-1}}\cong\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{\widehat{n-s-1}}w_{\widehat{n-s-1},0}\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{\widehat{n-s-1}}/\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{\widehat{n-s-1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{B}_{s}w_{0,s}\mathbf{B}_{s}/\mathbf{B}_{s},

where
w_{0,s}
is the longest element in
\mathscr{W}_{n}
. Therefore, we obtain a map
\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\rightarrow{\mathbf{T}}_{s}\backslash\mathbf{B}_{s}w_{0,s}\mathbf{B}_{s}/\mathbf{B}_{s}
that sends
[g\mathbf{B}]
to
[p_{\widehat{n-s-1}}(u_{0}^{-1}g\mathbf{B})]
. By the non-critical assumption, this map factors through

p_{\mathrm{cr}}:\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\rightarrow\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}^{\mathrm{{cr}}}.\;

(3.20)

Recall the natural morphism
p_{\mathrm{ref}}:\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\rightarrow\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},S_{0}(u)}(S_{0}(u))
in (
3.4
). Then Theorem
3.10
is equivalent to the following theorem.

Theorem 3.12
.

The following morphism is injective:

\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\xrightarrow{(p_{\mathrm{ref}},p_{\mathrm{cr}})}\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},S_{0}(u)}(S_{0}(u))\times\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}^{\mathrm{{cr}}}.\;

(3.21)

Moreover, consider the natural morphism:

g_{S_{0}}^{\circ}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0}(u)})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}}).

(3.22)

Denote the image of
g_{S_{0}}^{\circ}
by

\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})^{\circ}_{S_{0}}:=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0}(u)})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}}).

Then we have an exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\ker(g_{S_{0}}^{\circ})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0}(u)})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})^{\circ}_{S_{0}}\rightarrow 0

(3.23)

Theorem
3.11
can be reformulated as the following Lie-algebras statement:

Theorem 3.13
.

\ker(g_{S_{0}}^{\circ})
and
\big\{({\mathfrak{l}}_{S_{0}(u)}\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u^{-1}g}({\mathfrak{b}}))_{{}_{/\mathrm{Ad}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}(u)})}}\big\}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}
determine
[g\mathbf{B}]\in\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0}).\;

Moreover, consider the following natural surjection:

\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},S_{0}(u)}(S_{0}(u))={\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}(u)}\backslash(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{S_{0}(u)})w_{0,S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{S_{0}(u)})/(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{S_{0}(u)})\twoheadrightarrow\prod_{j\in S_{0}(u)}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}\backslash(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})w_{0,\{j\}}(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})/(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j}),

(3.24)

which extracts the Hodge parameters of each
2\times 2
block in
\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},S_{0}(u)}(S_{0}(u))
(in the language of
[
16
]
, these are called simple Hodge parameters). For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, we obtain maps:

\displaystyle p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref},u}

\displaystyle:\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\rightarrow\prod_{j\in S_{0}(u)}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}\backslash(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})w_{0,\{j\}}(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})/(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j}),

(3.25)

\displaystyle p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref}}:=\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref},u}

\displaystyle:\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\rightarrow\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\prod_{j\in S_{0}(u)}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}\backslash(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})w_{0,\{j\}}(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})/(\mathbf{L}^{\mathbf{B}}_{j})

Denote by
\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},n}(S_{0})^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ST}}
the image of
p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref}}
. Let
[\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})]^{\Delta}
be the image of
[\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})]
under the morphism

(p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref}},p_{\mathrm{cr}}):\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\rightarrow\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},n}(S_{0})^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ST}}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}^{\mathrm{{cr}}}.

Corollary 3.14
.

If
\max_{1\leq i\leq s}l_{i}\leq 2
, then
\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},\Delta}(S_{0})\cong\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},n}(S_{0})^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ST}}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{nc},s}^{\mathrm{{cr}}}
.

4
Deformations of semistable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

4.1
Deformations of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{S_{0}(u)}
for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
) be the subspace of trianguline deformations (resp.,
\mathbf{P}_{S_{0}(u)}
-parabolic deformations) of
\mathbf{D}
with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
(resp.,
\mathcal{F}_{S_{0}(u)}
), i.e.,
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
(resp.
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
) if and only if

\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}=\widetilde{R}_{u,1}-\widetilde{R}_{u,2}-\cdots-\widetilde{R}_{u,n},\;\text{resp.,\;}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}=\widetilde{E}_{u,1}-\widetilde{E}_{u,2}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{u,f_{u}}

where
\widetilde{R}_{u,l}\cong{R}_{u,l}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{l}\epsilon)
for
\psi_{l}\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
(resp.,
\widetilde{E}_{u,i}
is a deformation of
{E}_{u,i}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
). We further define a subspace
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
, i.e.,
\widetilde{D}=[\widetilde{E}_{u,1}-\widetilde{E}_{u,2}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{u,f_{u}}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
belongs to
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
if and only if
\widetilde{E}_{u,i}\cong{E}_{u,i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}^{\prime}\epsilon)
for
\psi^{\prime}_{i}\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
. We have natural maps:

\displaystyle\kappa_{u}

\displaystyle:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E),\widetilde{D}\mapsto(\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}

(4.1)

\displaystyle\kappa_{S_{0}(u)}

\displaystyle:{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}(u)}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E),\;\widetilde{D}\mapsto(\psi^{\prime}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq f_{u}}.

Note that
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
and
i\in\Delta
, we define a subspace
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u,[i]}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
of
{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
, i.e.,
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
belongs to
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u,[i]}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
if
\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{R}_{u,1}-\cdots-\widetilde{R}_{u,i-1}-\widetilde{R_{u,i}^{i+1}}-\widetilde{R}_{u,i+2}-\cdots-\widetilde{R}_{u,n}
, where
\widetilde{R}_{u,l}\cong{R}_{u,l}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{l}\epsilon)
for
\psi_{l}\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
and
l\neq i,i+1
, and
\widetilde{R_{u,i}^{i+1}}
is a deformation of
R_{u,i}^{i+1}
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
. Note that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\{i\},u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
.

Let
R^{+}_{u}:=\{(i,j):1\leq i<j\leq n,u^{-1}(j)=u^{-1}(i)+1,u^{-1}(i)\in I_{0}\}
. Put

{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E):=\left\{(\psi_{i})\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\;|\;\psi_{j}-\psi_{i}\in\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j},\forall\;(i,j)\in R^{+}_{u}\right\}.

(4.2)

where
\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}
is the so-called simple
\mathscr{L}
-invariant in
[
16
]
; it is a codimension-
1
subspace of
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
and depends on
u,i,j
. Moreover,
E\val_{p}\neq\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}
for
u^{-1}(i)\in S_{0}
, while
E\val_{p}=\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}
for
u^{-1}(i)\in I_{0}\backslash S_{0}
. See
[
22
, Appendix-I, Proposition 6.3]
for its inductive definition. In what follows, we do not need the precise description. Recall the inclusion
j_{S_{0}}:\mathscr{W}_{s}\rightarrow\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
(see the argument around (
2.6
)). Put
w_{0}^{(S_{0})}:=j_{S_{0}}(w_{0,s})
. It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{1}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)=2n-|I_{0}|,\quad\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{w_{0}^{(S_{0})}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)=2n-|S_{0}|.

(4.3)

Proposition 4.1
.

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})=1+\frac{n(n+1)}{2}
and
\kappa_{u}
is a surjection onto
{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
.

Proof.

We first compute
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. We follow the route in the proof of

[
16
, Proposition 3.6]
. Let
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\mathbf{D})
be the saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
{\rm End}(\mathbf{D})
that preserves the triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Then we have a natural exact sequence of

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
:

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}(\mathcal{R}_{E}(\delta_{n}),\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1}}}(\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1})\rightarrow 0.

Note that
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}(\mathcal{R}_{E}(\delta_{n}),\mathbf{D}))=0
by non-split assumption. By induction on
n
, we show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\mathbf{D}))\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1}}}(\mathbf{D}_{1}^{n-1}))\cong E
. It suffices to show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\mathbf{D}))=0
. By induction on
n
, we only need to show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}({\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}(\mathcal{R}_{E}(\delta_{n}),\mathbf{D}))=0
. By Tate duality, we have isomorphism
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}(\mathcal{R}_{E}(\delta_{n}),\mathbf{D}))\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}(\mathbf{D},\mathcal{R}_{E}(\delta_{n})))
. If
i-1\not\in I_{0}
, the result is obvious. If
i-1\in I_{0}
, we still return to the case in
[
16
, Proposition 3.6]
. The same route as in
[
22
, Appendix-I, Proposition 6.3]
proves the second assertion.
∎

Put
{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E):=\{\psi\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E):\exists\;\psi_{0}\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E),\text{s.t.\;}\psi-\psi_{0}\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\}
. Note that
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)=s+1
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
and
\ast\in\{{\rm sm},g^{\prime}\}
, set

{\rm Hom}_{\ast,u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E):={\rm Hom}_{\ast}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\cap{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E).

We have
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm},1}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)=s
. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g^{\prime},u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
) be the preimage of
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm},u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime},u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
) under
\kappa_{u}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
be the subspace of de Rham deformations of
\mathbf{D}
.

(1)

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})={\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. Moreover, we have

\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})=1+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I_{0}\backslash S_{0}|
.

(2)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})={\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
.

Proof.

By
[
17
, Appendix, Proposition A.3]
, for any
i<j
, we deduce
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}({\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}},W(R_{1,i}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}{R_{1,j}}))=0
, where
W(-)
means the associated
E
-
B
pair. The dévissage argument shows the inclusion
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. Therefore,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})={\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
by definition. Since
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm},1}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
has codimension
2n-|I_{0}|-s=n-|I_{0}|-|S_{0}|
in
{\rm Hom}_{1}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
, we get that
\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})=1+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I_{0}\backslash S_{0}|
. It remains to prove
(2)
. Since
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon)
is de Rham iff
\psi_{i}\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}_{p},E)
, we deduce that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. On the other hand, for
(\psi_{i})\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
, we will show that

\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}=\widetilde{R}_{u,1}-\widetilde{R}_{u,2}-\cdots-\widetilde{R}_{u,n},\;

with
\widetilde{R}_{u,i}\cong R_{u,i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon)
and
\psi\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}_{p},E)
de Rham. It suffices to prove, by induction on
j
, that
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{j}:=\widetilde{R}_{1}-\widetilde{R}_{2}-\cdots-\widetilde{R}_{j}
is de Rham. The case
j=1
is obvious. Assume
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{j}
is de Rham, and prove that
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{j+1}
is de Rham. It suffices to show that each element in

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}\left({\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}},W(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime})\right),\;\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}R_{u,j+1}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}(1-\psi_{j+1}\epsilon)

is de Rham. Since all the Hodge-Tate weights of
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}
are positive, we deduce from
[
17
, Proposition A.3]
that
{\mathrm{H}}_{g}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}},W(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}))={\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}},W(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}))
(note that
\widetilde{{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}({\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}},W(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}))={{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}({\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}},W(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}))
). We thus obtain
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
.
∎

The following lemma is an analogue of
[
15
, Lemma 2.11]
.

Lemma 4.3
.

For any
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, put
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,\{u_{1},u_{2}\}}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D}):={\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u_{1}}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u_{2}}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. Then the following diagram commutes:

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.30435pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern (4.4)

where
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm},\{u_{1},u_{2}\}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E):={\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm},u_{1}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\cap{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm},u_{2}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
.

For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D}):=\ker\kappa_{u}\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
and
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D}):=\ker\kappa_{1}
. Then we have
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. As a corollary, we obtain

Corollary 4.4
.

For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, we have
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})={\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. Moreover, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})=1+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I_{0}\backslash S_{0}|-s
.

For any subspace
V\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
, we put
\overline{V}:=V/V\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
.

4.2
Reinterpretation and supplements for deformations

We briefly recall Fontaine’s theory of almost de Rham representations. Let
B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}:=B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}[\log t]
and
B_{{\rm pdR}}:=B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}[1/t]
. The
{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
-action on
B_{{\rm dR}}
extends uniquely to an action on
B_{{\rm pdR}}
with
g(\log t)=\log t+\log({\epsilon}(g))
. Let
v_{{\rm pdR}}
denote the unique
B_{{\rm dR}}
-linear derivation of
B_{{\rm pdR}}
such that
v_{{\rm pdR}}(\log t)=-1
. Note that
v_{{\rm pdR}}
commutes with
{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
and that both preserve
B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}
. Let
W
(resp.,
W^{+}
) be a
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representation (resp.,
B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}
-representation) of
{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
that is free of finite rank. Let
D_{{\rm pdR}}(W):=(B_{{\rm pdR}}\otimes_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W)^{{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}}
, which is a finite-dimensional
L
-vector space of dimension at most
\dim_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W
. The
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representation
W
is called
almost de Rham
if
\dim_{L}D_{{\rm pdR}}(W)=\dim_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W
.
W^{+}
is called
almost de Rham
if
W^{+}[1/t]
is almost de Rham.

Keep the notation of Section
2.2
. In this section, we use the language of
E
-
B
-pairs (see
[
24
]
). Recall that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:=\mathbf{D}[1/t]
over
\mathcal{R}_{E}[1/t]
admits a triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{u}
with graded pieces
R_{u,i}[1/t]=\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{u^{-1}(i)}))[1/t]
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Let
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}=W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}:=W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\mathbf{D})
) be the
\mathbf{B}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation (resp.,
\mathbf{B}_{\rm dR}^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation) of
{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
associated to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.
\mathbf{D}
). Note that
D\cong D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
induces a complete flag:

{\mathbf{F}}_{u}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{n}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}

on
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
by
(\mathbf{B}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)
-subrepresentations of
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
. Applying the functor
D_{{\rm pdR}}(-)
, we obtain the corresponding complete flag
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)
on
D
:

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)

\displaystyle:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{n}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)=D

(4.5)

Moreover, the
B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-lattice
{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}
induces the Hodge filtration
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}(D)
:

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}(D)

\displaystyle:\ 0\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n}}_{H}(D)\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n-1}}_{H}(D)\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{1}}}_{H}(D)=D

(4.6)

with
{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{n+1-i}}_{H}(D):=\big(t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n+1-i}}D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}})\big)^{{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}}
.

The discussion in this section is similar to
[
5
, Section 2.4]
and
[
22
, Section 3.3]
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, let
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D,D)
) be the subspace of endomorphisms of the
E
-vector space
D
that preserve the filtration
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{\bullet}(D)
(resp.,
{\rm Fil}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}^{\bullet}(D)
). Put

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D,D):={\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)\cap{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D,D).

By the same argument as in the discussion before
[
5
, (112)]
, we obtain a canonical
E
-linear morphism:

\displaystyle\nu:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

(4.7)

By
[
5
, Lemma 2.4.1]
,
\nu
has kernel
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. Moreover, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, the map (
4.7
) induces

\displaystyle\nu_{u}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})/{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g,u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D,D)

(4.8)

For
1\leq i\leq s
, put
D^{i}:=D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{E_{i}})
. Similar to
[
22
, Lemma 3.13]
, we obtain

Lemma 4.5
.

(1)

For
1\leq i\leq s
, we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0}

(4.9)

where
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})=1+\frac{r_{i}(r_{i}-1)}{2}
and

.

(2)

For
1\leq i\leq s
,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
, and we have a natural isomorphism (obtained by inverting
t
)
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])
.

In the sequel, define (recall that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:=\mathbf{D}[1/t]
)

{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}):=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t]).\;

(4.10)

Lemma 4.6
.

(1)

There is an isomorphism

{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}).

(4.11)

(2)

u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, the following diagram commutes:

(4.12)

Moreover,
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu)=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-|I_{0}\backslash S_{0}|
.

Proof.

Recall the dimensions of
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
and

from Proposition (

) and Corollary

. These subspaces yield a natural morphism

Since
\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})=s

. Moreover,
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu)=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-|I_{0}\backslash S_{0}|
.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

We have
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}\cong\oplus_{i=1}^{s}E_{i}[1/t]
.

Proof.

We only need to use the fact that
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{e}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha))[1/t])={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha))[1/t])=0
if
\alpha\neq 1
, and
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\alpha))[1/t])\cong E
if
\alpha=p
, by
[
24
, Lemmas 4.2 & 4.3]
.
∎

Similar to
[
5
, (121)]
and the commutative diagram below
[
5
, (123)]
, we have the natural maps

{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{E}(D,D).

, (122)-(123)]
, we have a commutative diagram of short exact sequences:

(4.16)

With respect to the basis
e_{1},\cdots,e_{n}
of
D

{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then for each

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D,D)\cong\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{b}}),\;{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)=\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}}),\;{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D,D)=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{b}})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}}).

(4.17)

Remark 4.9
.

The non-critical assumption implies that
g\mathbf{B}=ub_{u}w_{0}\mathbf{B}
for some
b_{u}\in\mathbf{B}
. Note that
\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}=\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0}}({\mathfrak{b}})
is the Borel algebra of lower triangular matrices. Then
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{b}})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})\cong\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{u}}({\mathfrak{b}}\cap\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}})=\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{u}}({\mathfrak{t}})
.

We can say more about
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})
. Note that
{\mathfrak{b}}={\mathfrak{t}}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset}
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Under the identifications in Remark
4.9
, we have
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset})
.

Proof.

The lemma follows from the definition of the Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathscr{L}
-invariants
\{\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}\}_{(i,j)\in R_{u}^{+}}
and the Colmez–Greenberg–Stevens formula (see
[
16
, Proposition 3.5]
), which implies that
\mathrm{coker}(\nu_{u})
only comes from
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{t}})
.
∎

Let
{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{S_{0}}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
be the image of the following map:

g_{\mathbf{D}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D}).\;
