5
Higher extension groups and Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants

In
[
28
]
, we see that higher extension groups (between locally analytic generalized Steinberg representations) play a crucial role in identifying the automorphic counterparts of
p
-adic Hodge parameters for Steinberg
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules.

Let
D(G,E)
be the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic distribution algebra. For admissible locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations
V_{1},V_{2}
, the continuous strong duals
V_{1}^{\vee}
and
V_{2}^{\vee}
are coadmissible modules over
D(G,E)
. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2}):={\rm Ext}^{1}_{D(G,E)}(V_{2}^{\vee},V_{1}^{\vee})
, where the latter extension group is defined in the abelian category of abstract
D(G,E)
-modules. In this case,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2})
coincides with the extension group in the category of admissible locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations. Suppose that
V_{1},V_{2}
have the same central character
\chi
. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}(V_{1},V_{2})
be the subspace of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2})
consisting of locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic extensions with central character
\chi
.

Let
I\subseteq\Delta
. Suppose that
\Delta\backslash I=\{d_{1},\cdots,d_{r-1}\}
. Put
d_{0}=0
and
d_{r}=n
. Then
\mathbf{L}_{I}={\rm GL}_{d_{1}-d_{0}}\times{\rm GL}_{d_{2}-d_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{d_{r}-d_{r-1}}
. For
1\leq j\leq r
, write
\mathbf{L}_{I,j}:={\rm GL}_{d_{j}-d_{j-1}}
. Let
\Delta_{I,j}\subseteq\Delta
be the simple roots of
\mathbf{L}_{I,j}
. Thus
J:=\sqcup_{1\leq j\leq r}\Delta_{I,j}
. For
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
, let
\eta^{I^{\prime}}_{I}
be the square root of the modulus character of
{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{I}\cap{\mathbf{I}}_{I^{\prime}}
in
\mathbf{L}_{I^{\prime}}
. If
I^{\prime}=\Delta
(
I=\emptyset
), we omit the superscript
I^{\prime}
(resp., subscript
I
). In particular,
\eta_{I}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}|\mathrm{det}_{{\rm GL}_{d_{i}-d_{i-1}}}|^{\frac{1}{2}(-\sum_{j=i+1}^{r}(d_{j}-d_{j-1})+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(d_{j}-d_{j-1}))}
.

Let
d
be an integer, and let
{\mathbf{T}}_{d}
(resp.,
\mathbf{B}_{d}
) be the standard diagonal torus (resp., Borel subgroup) of
{\rm GL}_{d}
and let
\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{d}
be the Borel subgroup opposite to
\mathbf{B}_{d}
. For
I\subseteq\Delta_{d}
and
?\in\{\infty,{\rm an}\}
, set (
[
16
, Section 2.1.2]
)

v^{?}_{I,\Delta_{d}}:=i^{?}_{I,\Delta_{d}}/\sum_{I\subsetneq J}i^{?}_{J,\Delta_{d}},\;i^{?}_{I,\Delta_{d}}:=\left({\rm Ind}^{{\rm GL}_{d}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{d}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}1_{{\mathbf{T}}_{d}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\right)^{?}.

\{v^{{\rm an}}_{I,\Delta_{d}}\}_{I\subseteq\Delta_{d}}
(resp.,
\{v^{\infty}_{I,\Delta_{d}}\}_{I\subseteq\Delta_{d}}
) are the so-called locally analytic (resp., smooth) generalized Steinberg representations of
{\rm GL}_{d}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. We write
{\rm St}^{?}_{d}:=v^{?}_{\emptyset,\Delta_{d}}
, which we call the locally analytic (resp., smooth) Steinberg representation.

5.1
On
{\rm Ext}^{\bullet}
between parabolic inductions of locally analytic generalized Steinberg representations

Fix
J\subseteq\Delta
. Write
\mathbf{L}_{J}\cong\mathbf{L}_{J,1}\times\mathbf{L}_{J,2}\times\cdots\times\mathbf{L}_{J,d}
and
J=\sqcup_{1\leq j\leq d}\Delta_{J,j}
(so that
d=|\Delta\backslash J|+1
). Fix
{\mathbf{a}}:=(a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{d})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. For
I\subseteq J
, put

v^{{\rm an}}_{I,J,\Delta}({\mathbf{a}}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{J}}v^{{\rm an}}_{I,J}({\mathbf{a}})\right)^{{\rm an}},\;v^{{\rm an}}_{I,J}({\mathbf{a}}):=\widehat{\boxtimes}_{j=1}^{d}|\cdot|^{a_{j}}v^{{\rm an}}_{\Delta_{J,j}\cap I,\Delta_{J,j}}

(5.1)

The locally analytic representation
v^{{\rm an}}_{I,J}({\mathbf{a}})
admits the following Bruhat-Tits resolution
{\mathbf{C}}_{I,J}({\mathbf{a}})
(see
[
28
, (463)]
):

\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{J}}i^{{\rm an}}_{J,J}({\mathbf{a}})\right)^{{\rm an}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\bigoplus_{I\subseteq{K}\subseteq J,|K|=l}\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{K}}i^{{\rm an}}_{K,J}({\mathbf{a}})\right)^{{\rm an}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{J}}i^{{\rm an}}_{I,J}({\mathbf{a}})\right)^{{\rm an}}\rightarrow v^{{\rm an}}_{I,J,\Delta}({\mathbf{a}}),

(5.2)

where the middle term lies in
-l
degree and
i^{{\rm an}}_{I,J}({\mathbf{a}}):=\widehat{\boxtimes}_{j=1}^{d}|\cdot|^{a_{j}}i^{{\rm an}}_{\Delta_{J,j}\cap I,\Delta_{J,j}}
. Thus, for any
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
, we have

M^{\bullet}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}):={\rm Ext}^{\bullet}_{G,Z}({\mathbf{C}}_{I_{1},J}({\mathbf{a}}),{\mathbf{C}}_{I_{2},J}({\mathbf{a}}))={\rm Ext}^{\bullet-|I_{1}\backslash I_{2}|}_{G,Z}(v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{1},J,\Delta}({\mathbf{a}}),v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{2},J,\Delta}({\mathbf{a}})).\;

(5.3)

By the same argument as in
[
28
, Lemma 3.3.2
\&
Proposition 3.3.3]
, the extension group
M^{\bullet}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
can be identified with (see
[
28
, (593)]
and keep its notation)

M^{\bullet}_{I_{1},I_{2}}:={\rm Ext}^{\bullet}_{G,Z}({\mathbf{C}}_{I_{1},J},{\mathbf{C}}_{I_{2},J}).\;

(5.4)

Applying
[
28
, Theorem 4.5.2]
to the case
(I_{0},I_{1})=(I_{1},J)
and
(I_{2},I_{3})=(I_{2},J)
, we deduce (keep the notation in
[
28
, Theorem 4.5.2]
)

Theorem 5.1
.

Set
J^{\prime}=I_{2}\cup(J\backslash I_{1})\subseteq J
. We have the following results.

(i)

If
{\rm Ext}^{h}_{G,Z}(v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{1},J}({\mathbf{a}}),v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{2},J}({\mathbf{a}}))\neq 0
, then
|I_{1}\backslash I_{2}|\leq h\leq n^{2}-n+|J|-|I_{1}\backslash I_{2}|
.

(ii)

Define the bottom extension group
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}):={\rm Ext}_{G,Z}^{|I_{1}\backslash I_{2}|}(v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{1},J,\Delta}({\mathbf{a}}),v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{2},J,\Delta}({\mathbf{a}}))
. The space
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
admits a canonical decreasing filtration

0={\rm Fil}^{-|J^{\prime}|+1}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))\subseteq{\rm Fil}^{-|J^{\prime}|}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))\subseteq\cdots\subseteq{\rm Fil}^{-|J|}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))={\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})

(5.5)

with graded piece
{\rm Fil}^{-l}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))/{\rm Fil}^{-l+1}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))\cong E_{2,J^{\prime},J}^{-l,l+|J|-2|J^{\prime}|}
admitting a basis induced from
\Psi_{J^{\prime},J,l}
for
|J^{\prime}|\leq l\leq|J|
.

For
1\leq j\leq d
and
I_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq I_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{J,j}
, we put

{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1}^{\prime},I_{2}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{a}})_{j}:={\rm Ext}_{\mathbf{L}_{J,j}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),Z}^{|I_{2}^{\prime}\backslash I_{1}^{\prime}|}(v^{{\rm an}}_{I^{\prime}_{1},\Delta_{J,j}},v^{{\rm an}}_{I_{2}^{\prime},\Delta_{J,j}}).

For
I_{3}\subseteq I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}
, we have a natural cup product map:

\kappa_{I_{1},I_{3}}^{I_{2}}:{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})\otimes_{E}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{2},I_{3}}({\mathbf{a}})\xrightarrow{\cup}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{3}}({\mathbf{a}}).

(5.6)

Using
[
28
, Theorem 5.3.6]
, we get that:

Theorem 5.2
.

The cup product map
\kappa_{I_{1},I_{3}}^{I_{2}}
is injective. If
I_{1}\backslash I_{2}
and
I_{2}\backslash I_{3}
are not connected (i.e., for any
i\in I_{1}\backslash I_{2}
and
i^{\prime}\in I_{2}\backslash I_{3}
, we have
|i-i^{\prime}|\geq 2
), then
\kappa_{I_{1},I_{3}}^{I_{2}}
is an isomorphism. Moreover, we have an isomorphism

{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1}\cap\Delta_{J,j},I_{2}\cap\Delta_{J,j}}({\mathbf{a}})_{j}.

Proof.

The first statement is
[
28
, Theorem 5.3.6]
. For the second statement, choose a sequence

I_{1}=T_{0}\subseteq T_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq T_{d}=I_{2}

such that
T_{l}\backslash T_{l-1}=(I_{2}\backslash I_{1})\cap\Delta_{J,l}
for
1\leq l\leq d
. Then for any
1\leq l\leq d
, the map

\kappa_{T_{0},T_{l}}^{T_{l-1}}:{\mathbb{E}}_{T_{0},T_{l-1}}({\mathbf{a}})\otimes_{E}{\mathbb{E}}_{T_{l-1},T_{l}}({\mathbf{a}})\xrightarrow{\cup}{\mathbb{E}}_{T_{0},T_{l}}({\mathbf{a}})

is an isomorphism By
[
28
, Theorem 1.3.5 (i)]
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}_{T_{l-1},T_{l}}\cong{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1}\cap\Delta_{J,j},I_{2}\cap\Delta_{J,j}}({\mathbf{a}})_{j}
. By applying this argument step by step, we get the desired tensor product decomposition.
∎

This theorem reduces
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
to the higher extension groups of its Levi (and thus Steinberg) blocks. In particular, we can describe the
cup product structure and the so-called Coxeter filtration of
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
. We collect (and review) them in the following proposition, see
[
28
, Lemmas 5.4.1
\&
5.4.4]
.

Proposition 5.3
.

(1)

{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})\cong{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1}\backslash I_{2},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})
.

(2)

For each
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
satisfying
\#I_{1}\backslash I_{2}=1
, there is a canonical isomorphism

{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E).\;

(3)

For each
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
satisfying
\#I_{1}\backslash I_{2}>1
, we set

{\mathbb{E}}^{<}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}):=\sum_{I_{3}\subsetneq I_{2}\subsetneq I_{1}}\im(\kappa_{I_{1},I_{3}}^{I_{2}})\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}).

Then
\dim_{E}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})/{\mathbb{E}}^{<}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})=t
, where
t:=\#\{1\leq l\leq d:(I_{1}\backslash I_{2})\cap\Delta_{J,l}\text{\;is an interval}\}
.

Let
\Phi_{J}^{+}
be the set of positive roots of
\mathbf{L}_{J}
with respect to
(\mathbf{L}_{J}\cap\mathbf{B},\mathbf{L}_{J}\cap{\mathbf{T}})
. For
\alpha=x_{i}-x_{j}\in\Phi^{+}_{J}
, put
I_{\alpha}=\{i,\cdots,j-1\}\subseteq J
. If
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}_{J}\backslash J
(i.e.,
|I_{\alpha}|\geq 2
), we fix a choice of
x_{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{\alpha},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})
such that its image in
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{\alpha},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})/{\mathbb{E}}^{<}_{I_{\alpha},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})
is non-zero, and we set
\overline{X}_{\alpha}:=\{x_{\alpha}\}
. If
\alpha\in J
(i.e.,
|I_{\alpha}|=1
), we write
x^{\infty}_{\alpha}
(resp.,
x_{\alpha}
) for the elements in
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{\alpha},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
corresponding to
\val_{p}
(resp.,
\log_{p}
). In this case, we put

\overline{X}_{\alpha}:=\{x^{\infty}_{\alpha},x_{\alpha}\}
. Moreover, for
\alpha=x_{i}-x_{j}
, we set
x^{\infty}_{\alpha}:=x^{\infty}_{\alpha_{i}}\cup\cdots\cup x^{\infty}_{\alpha_{j-1}}\in{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{\alpha},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})
.

For
I\subseteq J
, we write
\mathcal{S}_{I}
for the set of subsets
S\subseteq\Phi^{+}_{J}
such that
\sum_{\alpha\in S}\alpha=\alpha_{I}
. It is clear that there exists a bijection between
\mathcal{S}_{I}
and the set of partitions of
I
into non-empty subintervals (i.e., sending
S
to the partition
I=\sqcup_{\alpha\in S}I_{\alpha}
). For
S\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{I}
, we set
\overline{X}_{S}:=\prod_{\alpha\in S}\overline{X}_{\alpha}
. Then we have (similar to
[
28
, Proposition 5.4.3]
):

Proposition 5.4
.

For
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
,
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
admits a basis of the form
X_{I_{1},I_{2}}:=\sqcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{I_{1}\backslash I_{2}}}\overline{X}_{S}
.

For each
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
, if
|I_{1}\backslash I_{2}|=1
, we set
{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})=E\val_{p}\subseteq{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
. For general
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}
, we choose a sequence
I_{2}=J_{0}\subseteq{J_{1}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq{J_{t}}=I_{1}
such that
t:=|I_{1}\backslash I_{2}|
and
|{J_{j}}\backslash{J_{j-1}}|=1
for all
1\leq j\leq t
. We define
{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
as the image of the composition:

{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{{J_{t}},{J_{t-1}}}({\mathbf{a}})\otimes_{E}\cdots\otimes_{E}{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{{J_{1}},{J_{0}}}({\mathbf{a}})\hookrightarrow{{\mathbb{E}}}_{{J_{t}},{J_{t-1}}}({\mathbf{a}})\otimes_{E}\cdots\otimes_{E}{{\mathbb{E}}}_{{J_{1}},{J_{0}}}({\mathbf{a}})\xrightarrow{\cup}{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}),

which gives a canonical
E
-line in
{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
and is independent of the choice of the sequence
J_{0}\subseteq{J_{1}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq{J_{t}}
. We define
\widetilde{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
as the image of the injection

{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{J,I_{1}}({\mathbf{a}})\otimes_{E}{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})\otimes_{E}{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{I_{2},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})\xrightarrow{\cup}{{\mathbb{E}}}_{J,\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}}):={\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}}).

By choosing a non-zero element in
{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{J,I_{1}}({\mathbf{a}})
(resp.,
{{\mathbb{E}}}^{\infty}_{I_{2},\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})
), we obtain a non-canonical isomorphism

\iota_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}):{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}).

Similar to
[
28
, Definition 8.1.1]
, we can now define Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants. Put
{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}}):={{\mathbb{E}}}_{J,\emptyset}({\mathbf{a}})
for simplicity.

Definition 5.5
.

A Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariant is
{\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
such that:

(1)

{\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
is co-dimensional
1
in
{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
.

(2)

For each
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
,
{\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})\cap\widetilde{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})\subsetneq\widetilde{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})
. Let
{\mathbb{W}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}):=\iota^{-1}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})({\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})\cap\widetilde{{\mathbb{E}}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))
, then this condition implies
\dim_{E}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}/{\mathbb{W}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}})=1
.

(3)

For each
I_{3}\subseteq I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq J
, the composition
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}\otimes_{E}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{2},I_{3}}\xrightarrow{\cup}{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{3}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{3}}/{\mathbb{W}}_{I_{1},I_{3}}({\mathbf{a}})
factors through an isomorphism between
E
-lines

({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}/{\mathbb{W}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}))\otimes_{E}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{2},I_{3}}/{\mathbb{W}}_{I_{2},I_{3}}({\mathbf{a}}))\xrightarrow{\sim}({\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{3}}/{\mathbb{W}}_{I_{1},I_{3}}({\mathbf{a}})).

Let
\mathcal{BS}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
be the moduli space of Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants inside
{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
, which is a closed subvariety of a Zariski-open subvariety of the projective space
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}}))
. Similar to the argument in
[
28
, Theorem 8.1.4]
, for
{\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
and
\alpha\in\Psi_{J}^{+}
, there exists a unique
\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}({\mathbf{a}})\in E
such that
x_{\alpha}-\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}({\mathbf{a}})x^{\infty}_{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})_{I_{\alpha},\emptyset}
. This gives an element
(\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}({\mathbf{a}}))_{\alpha\in\Psi_{J}^{+}}\in{\mathfrak{l}}_{J}\cap{\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset}
. Indeed, the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{BS}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{l}}_{J}\cap{\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset},\;{\mathbb{W}}({\mathbf{a}})\mapsto(\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}({\mathbf{a}}))_{\alpha\in\Psi_{J}^{+}}.

(5.7)

is an isomorphism. The symbol
(\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}({\mathbf{a}}))_{\alpha\in\Psi_{J}^{+}}
might suggest that there exists a way to match Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants inside
{\mathbb{E}}_{J}({\mathbf{a}})
with the classical Fontaine-Mazur
\mathscr{L}
-invariants defined for Steinberg
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules.

5.2
Capture
p
-adic Hodge parameters through Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants

Recall
\underline{\phi}:=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1}p,\cdots,\alpha_{1}p^{l_{1}-1},\cdots,\alpha_{s},\alpha_{s}p,\cdots,\alpha_{s}p^{l_{s}-1}):=(\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{n})
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, recall

{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{n}{\rm unr}(\phi_{u^{-1}(j)}).

For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, we obtain a
\mathbf{P}_{S_{0}(u)}
-parabolic filtration
\mathcal{F}_{S_{0}(u)}
of
\mathbf{D}
with graded Steinberg pieces
\{E_{u,i}\}_{1\leq i\leq f_{u}}
. Note that
\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}(u)}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq f_{u}}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}(u),i}:=\prod_{1\leq i\leq f_{u}}{\rm GL}_{r_{u,i}}
. For
1\leq i\leq f_{u}
, it is easy to see that

{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta^{S_{0}(u)}|_{\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}(u),i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}={\rm unr}(a_{i}(u))\otimes\mathrm{1}_{{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}(u),i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}.

We apply the results in Section
5.1
to
{\mathbf{a}}={\mathbf{a}}(u):=(a_{1}(u),\cdots,a_{f_{u}}(u))
and
J=S_{0}(u)
. For any
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{S_{0}(u)}
, we rewrite
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}({\mathbf{a}}(u))
(resp.,
{\mathbb{E}}({\mathbf{a}}(u))
) with
{\mathbb{E}}_{I_{1},I_{2}}(u)
(resp.,
{\mathbb{E}}(u)
) for simplicity.

Definition 5.6
.

A total Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariant of monodromy type
S_{0}
is a collection
\{{\mathbb{W}}(u)\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}(u)\}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}
with
{\mathbb{W}}(u)
a Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariant in
{\mathbb{E}}(u)
(in the sense of Definition
5.5
). Let
\mathcal{BS}(u)
be the moduli space of Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants inside
{\mathbb{E}}(u)
. Then we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{BS}(u)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{l}}_{S_{0}(u)}\cap{\mathfrak{n}}_{\emptyset},\;{\mathbb{W}}(u)\mapsto(\mathscr{L}_{\alpha}(u))_{\alpha\in\Psi_{S_{0}(u)}^{+}},

(5.8)

Remark 5.7
.

We do not investigate here the relationships between
\{{\mathbb{W}}(u)\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}(u)\}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}
.

Proposition 5.8
.

The
p
-adic Hodge parameters of
\{E_{u,i}\}_{1\leq i\leq f_{u}}
are captured by certain Breuil-Schraen
\mathscr{L}
-invariants
{\mathbb{W}}(u)\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}(u)
. In particular, the collection
\{{\mathbb{W}}(u)\}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}
contains full information of
p_{\mathrm{ref}}(\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D}))
.

Remark 5.9
.

Via the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence, we expect that the conjectural locally analytic representation
\pi_{{\rm an}}(\mathbf{D})
has an “extension structure” that
comes from locally analytic parabolic inductions of locally analytic generalized Steinberg representations; see the two branchs of (
8.5
) for a
{\rm GL}_{3}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-example. This is another reason to consider
{\rm Ext}^{\bullet}
between such representations.

6
Locally analytic representations for semistable case

Throughout this section, fix a non-critical semistable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{D}
with Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}:={\mathbf{h}}_{\mathbf{D}}
and keep the notation in Section
2.2
. Recall the two subsets
S_{0}\subseteq I_{0}\subseteq\Delta
. Let
I_{0}\backslash S_{0}=\{l_{1}<l_{2}<\cdots<l_{m-1}\}
. For each
0\leq j\leq m-1
, let
n_{j}:=r_{l_{j}+1}=r_{l_{j}+2}=\cdots=r_{l_{j+1}}
, then
\mathbf{L}_{I_{0}}:={\rm GL}_{l_{1}n_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{(l_{2}-l_{1})n_{2}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{(l_{m}-l_{m-1})n_{m}}:=\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},1}\times\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},2}\times\cdots\times\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},m}
. For
1\leq j\leq m
, recall that
\Delta_{I_{0},j}
is the set of simple roots of
\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},j}
, so that
I_{0}:=\sqcup_{1\leq j\leq m}\Delta_{I_{0},j}
. For
1\leq j\leq m
, put
S_{0,j}:=\Delta_{I_{0},j}\cap S_{0}
.

For
I\subseteq\Delta
and
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, consider the smooth principal series of
\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I,u}(\underline{\phi})

\displaystyle:=\left({\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta^{I}\right)^{\infty}.

(6.1)

In particular, if
I=\{i\}
for
i\in\Delta
, we rewrite the subscript
\{i\}
with
i
for simplicity. If
I=\Delta
, we drop the symbol
\Delta
. The irreducible constituents of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{\phi})
(equivalently,
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{u}(\underline{\phi})
) are described as follows. Put
I=I_{0}
and
u=1
, then

\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I_{0},1}(\underline{\phi})\cong\boxtimes_{j=1}^{m}\Big({\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},j}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},j}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\big({\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta^{I_{0}}\big)|_{{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},j}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\Big)^{\infty}.

Note that
\big({\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta^{I_{0}}\big)|_{{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},j}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}={\rm unr}(\alpha_{j})\otimes\mathrm{1}_{{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I_{0},j}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}
for some
\alpha_{j}\in E^{\times}
by definition of
I_{0}
. For
J\subseteq I_{0}\subseteq I
, put

v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{\phi}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{m}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{j})\otimes_{E}v^{\infty}_{J\cap\Delta_{I_{0},j},\Delta_{I_{0},j}},\;v^{\infty}_{J,I}(\underline{\phi}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{I_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{\phi})\eta_{I_{0}}\right)^{\infty}.

Proposition 6.1
.

(1)

For
J\subseteq I_{0}
,
v^{\infty}_{J,\Delta}(\underline{\phi})
and
v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{\phi})
are irreducible. The Jordan–Hölder factors of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{\phi})
(resp.,
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1,I_{0}}(\underline{\phi})
) are
\{v^{\infty}_{J,\Delta}(\underline{\phi})\}_{J\subseteq I_{0}}
(resp.,
\{v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{\phi})\}_{J\subseteq I_{0}}
).

(2)

For any
I_{1},I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}\subseteq I
, there exists a unique multiplicity-free finite-length representation
Q^{\diamond}_{I}(I_{1},I_{2})
which has simple socle
v^{\infty}_{I_{1},I}(\underline{\phi})
and simple cosocle
v^{\infty}_{I_{2},I}(\underline{\phi})
.

Proof.

Part
(1)
follows from
[
31
, Theorem 9.7]
. It suffices to prove Part
(2)
. For
1\leq j\leq m
and
I^{\prime}_{j},J^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq\Delta_{I_{0},j}
, by
[
10
, Lemma 2.2.1]
, there exists a unique multiplicity free finite length representation
Q_{\Delta_{I_{0},j}}(I^{\prime}_{j},J^{\prime}_{j})
which has simple socle
v^{\infty}_{I^{\prime}_{j},\Delta_{I_{0},j}}
and simple cosocle
v^{\infty}_{J^{\prime}_{j},\Delta_{I_{0},j}}
. We put

\displaystyle Q^{\diamond}_{I_{0}}(I_{1},I_{2}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{m}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{j})\otimes_{E}Q_{\Delta_{I_{0},j}}(I_{1}\cap\Delta_{I_{0},j},I_{2}\cap\Delta_{I_{0},j}),\;

\displaystyle Q^{\diamond}_{I}(I_{1},I_{2}):=\Big({\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathbf{L}_{I}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}Q^{\diamond}_{I_{0}}(I_{1},I_{2})\eta^{I}_{I_{0}}\Big)^{\infty}.\;

The uniqueness argument is similar to the proof of
[
10
, Lemma 2.2.1]
.
∎

In particular, if
I_{1}=I_{2}=I^{\prime}
, we write
Q^{\diamond}_{I}(I^{\prime}):=Q^{\diamond}_{I}(I^{\prime},I^{\prime})
for simplicity. Note that
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{\phi})=Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(I_{0},\emptyset)
and
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{w_{0}^{(S_{0})}}(\underline{\phi})=Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(\emptyset,I_{0})
(recall
w_{0}^{(S_{0})}=j_{S_{0}}(w_{0,s})
, see the argument around (
2.6
)). The unique irreducible generic constituent of

\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{\phi})
is equal to
Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(\emptyset)
.

Let
\theta=(n-1,\cdots,0)\in X_{\Delta}^{+}
. Put
\lambda:=\lambda_{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{h}}_{\mathbf{D}}-\theta\in X_{\Delta}^{+}
. For any
I_{1},I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}
, put
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{1},I_{2},\lambda):=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{1},I_{2})\otimes_{E}L(\lambda)
, which is a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
G
. In the sequel, write

\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0},\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{0},\lambda).

For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
and
j\in\Delta
, put
\widehat{j}=\Delta\backslash\{j\}
. Let
\pi_{\widehat{j},u}(\underline{\phi})
be the unique generic irreducible constituent of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{\widehat{j},u}(\underline{\phi})
. Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
G
(see
[
25
, The main theorem]
):

C_{j,u}:=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{\widehat{j}}}\Big(\overline{L}(-s_{j}\cdot\underline{\lambda}),\pi_{\widehat{j},u}(\underline{\phi})\Big).

Lemma 6.2
.

For
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
,
C_{j,u}\cong C_{j,u^{\prime}}
if and only if
\{u(1),\cdots,u(j)\}=\{u^{\prime}(1),\cdots,u^{\prime}(j)\}
. In particular,
C_{j,u}
only depends on the coset
\mathscr{W}_{\widehat{j}}u
.

For
j\in\Delta
, we put
\mathcal{I}_{j}^{\sharp}:=\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}\cup\widehat{j}}
for simplicity and define
\mathcal{OS}^{1}_{{\rm gen}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\Big\{C_{j,u}:j\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}_{j}^{\sharp}\Big\}
. Note that
|\mathcal{OS}^{1}_{{\rm gen}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})|=2^{n}-2
. By
[
10
, Lemma 2.1.31]
, we have

Lemma 6.3
.

For
u\in\mathcal{I}_{j}^{\sharp}
and
j\in\Delta
, we have
\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{\widehat{j}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi_{\widehat{j},u}(\underline{\phi})\eta^{\widehat{j}}\Big)^{\infty}=Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-})
for
I_{u}^{+}:=D_{R}(w_{\widehat{j}}u)\cap I_{0}
and
I_{u}^{-}:=D_{R}(u)\cap I_{0}
.

6.1
Constructions of locally analytic representations

As a special case of
[
22
, Sections 4.2
\&
4.3]
, we have the following results.

Lemma 6.4
.

There is an isomorphism of
(n+1)
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)

(6.2)

The first term is isomorphic to
{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
j\in\Delta
and
u\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{j}
. For
I\in[I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-}]:=\{I\subseteq\Delta\;|\;I_{u}^{+}\cap I_{u}^{-}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{u}^{+}\cup I_{u}^{-}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(C_{j,u},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\right)=1,\;\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I,\lambda),C_{j,u}\right)=1,

(6.3)

which leads to a unique representation of the form
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)-C_{j,u}
(resp.,
C_{j,u}-Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I,\lambda)
). The same holds when
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
(resp.,
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I,\lambda)
) is replaced by
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
(resp.,
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
).

Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta\chi_{\lambda}\Big)^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}.\;

(6.4)

Note that the locally algebraic vectors in
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
are equal to

\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0}\backslash I_{u}^{-},I_{u}^{-},\lambda).

Let
\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. Note that the locally algebraic vectors
\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
in
\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
are equal to
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
. Let
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique subrepresentation of
\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
and such that all the irreducible constituents of
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})/\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
belong to
\mathcal{OS}^{1}_{{\rm gen}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
. Roughly speaking,
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
is the “first two layers” of the socle filtration of

\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
, i.e., the locally algebraic part
\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
plus the socle of the pure locally analytic part
\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})/\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
).

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
and
i\in\Delta
, fix a locally analytic representation
V_{i,u}
of
G
, which is isomorphic to a non-split extension of
C_{i,u}
by
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
(see Lemma
6.5
;
V_{i,u}
is unique up to a scalar), i.e.,

V_{i,u}:=[\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})-C_{i,u}],

and fix an injection
\iota_{i,u}:\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow V_{i,u}
. We have
C_{i,u}\cong V_{i,u}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i}}\cong{\rm GL}_{i}\times{\rm GL}_{n-i}
.

Proposition 6.6
.

For
i\in\Delta
, there is a canonical isomorphism of
(n+2)
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces associated to
(V_{i,u},\iota_{i,u})
:

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)}{\rm Hom}(\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u}\right).

(6.5)

The restriction of (
6.5
) to
{\rm Hom}(\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
only depends on
(V_{i,u},\iota_{i,u})
. Moreover, there is a short exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\Big)\xrightarrow{\iota_{i,u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u}\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(6.6)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, let
[u]_{j}\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{j}
be the minimal length representative in
\mathscr{W}_{\widehat{j}}u
. Consider the amalgamated sums:

\pi^{\flat}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}:=\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}_{j\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}(u)}V_{j,[u]_{j}}\hookrightarrow\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}:=\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}_{j\in\Delta}V_{j,[u]_{j}}.\;

We have the following short exact sequences of locally analytic representations:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi^{\flat}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\rightarrow\oplus_{j\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}(u)}C_{j,[u]_{j}}\rightarrow 0,

(6.7)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\rightarrow\oplus_{j\in\Delta}C_{j,[u]_{j}}\rightarrow 0.

For
i\in\Delta
, put
\mathcal{I}^{\flat}_{i}:=\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0},\emptyset}:i\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}(u)\}\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{i}
. For
\star\in\{\sharp,\flat\}
, consider the amalgamated sums:

\pi^{\star}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}_{j\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}^{\star}_{j}}V_{j,u}\cong\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\pi^{\star}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}.

We have the following short exact sequence of locally analytic representations:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi^{\star}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\oplus_{j\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}^{\star}_{j}}C_{j,u}\rightarrow 0.

(6.8)

\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
is a certain ”amalgamated sum” of
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
as
u
runs over
\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
. In particular, we have injections
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}^{\sharp}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}^{\sharp}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\pi^{\flat}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, we consider the natural map

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\big),\;

(6.9)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta\chi_{\lambda}\otimes_{E}(1+\psi\epsilon)\Big)^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}.

In particular, by Schraen’s spectral sequence
[
29
, (4.37)]
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})).

(6.10)

Then the natural quotient map
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
induces a map

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

By
[
16
, Lemma 2.26]
, this map factors through (induced by the injection
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})

then we obtain a map
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
. Composed with the pull-back map for the natural map

p_{u}:\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0},\emptyset,\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0}\backslash I_{u}^{-},\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0}\backslash I_{u}^{-},I_{u}^{-},\lambda)=\mathrm{PS}_{u}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),

we get a map:

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

(6.11)

\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
, we actually obtain a map:

Proposition 6.7
.

(1)

We have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}^{\sharp}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)=2n
. We have the following exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)

(6.12)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\oplus_{j\in\Delta}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{j,[u]_{j}}\right)\rightarrow 0.

Thus, we get an isomorphism
\zeta_{u}:{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)

(2)

For
?\in\{\flat,\sharp\}
, we have the following exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rightarrow\oplus_{j\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}^{?}_{j}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{j,u}\right)\rightarrow 0.

In particular, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)=n+1+(2^{n}-2)
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, recall the
\mathbf{P}_{S_{0}(u)}
-parabolic filtration

that determines
\{\mathscr{L}^{\Delta}(E_{u,i})\}

\pi^{\Delta}_{{\rm an}}(E_{u,i})=\Big[\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi}_{E_{u,i}},{\mathbf{h}}_{E_{u,i}})-\oplus_{j\in\Delta_{S_{0}(u),i}}Q_{\Delta_{S_{0}(u),i}}(j,\lambda_{E_{u,i}})\Big],

such that the non-split extension

encodes the information of

, where

.

Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}^{\Delta}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{S_{0}(u)}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi^{\Delta}_{{\rm an}}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta_{S_{0}(u)}\right)^{{\rm an}},\;\pi^{\Delta}_{{\rm an}}(\underline{\phi}^{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{f_{u}}\pi^{\Delta}_{{\rm an}}(E_{u,j}).\;

Note that
\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi^{\Diamond}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
.

For

, put

.

(1)

Remark 6.9
.

Similar to
[
5
, Proposition 2.2.7]
, we can also show that

does not depends on the choices of

.

For
i\in\Delta
, define
D^{\star,(i)}:=E\langle e_{t}:t\notin I^{c}_{u,i},\forall u\in\mathcal{I}^{\star}_{i}\rangle
and
D^{\star}_{(i)}=E\langle e_{t}:t\in I^{c}_{u,i}\text{\;for some\;}u\in\mathcal{I}^{\star}_{i}\rangle\subseteq D
. Thus
D=D^{\star,(i)}\oplus D^{\star}_{(i)}
. For
0\leq i\leq n-1
, recall that
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-i}}(D)=\oplus_{l=n-i}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{l}
(see (
3.3
)). Similar to
[
22
, Theorem 4.28]
, we have

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{i}
be the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D/D^{\star,(i)}=D^{\star}_{(i)}
. Then
\ker(t^{\star}_{D})
determines
\{\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{i}\}_{i\in\Delta}
when modulo the action of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}
.

Remark 6.11
.

By Theorem
6.8
, we get the dimension of
\ker(t^{\star}_{D})
. Moreover,
\ker(t^{\flat}_{D})
determines the “crystalline” Hodge parameter between the Steinberg blocks in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{ST}}
. In general, the kernel
\ker(t^{\sharp}_{D})
contains more information of Hodge parameters than
\ker(t^{\flat}_{D})
, since the irreducible constituents
V_{i,u}
that come from Steinberg blocks may contribute the kernel.

Consider the following composition

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{D}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u}\kappa_{u}}\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u}\zeta_{u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

(6.21)

The same strategy as in
[
5
, Proposition 2.5.5, Corollary 2.5.6]
and
[
22
, Propositions 4.24 & 4.25]
gives:

Theorem 6.12
.

Under the splitting in Proposition
4.8
,
t^{\sharp}_{D}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{D}}|_{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})}
is equal to
f_{\mathbf{D}}\circ\iota_{u}
, where
\iota_{u}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\hookrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
is the natural inclusion. In particular, the map
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\twoheadrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
factors through
\gamma_{\mathbf{D}}
, and
g_{\mathbf{D}}=t^{\sharp}_{D}\circ\gamma_{\mathbf{D}}
.

Let
\pi^{-,\star}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
be the unique quotient of the tautological extension of
\ker(t^{\star}_{D})\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. Similar to
[
5
, Corollary 2.2.13]
, we can show that
\pi^{-,\star}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
does not depends on any choices of
(\epsilon_{i,u})_{i\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and
\log_{p}(p)
. Finally, put

\pi^{-}_{\min}(\mathbf{D}):=\pi^{-,\flat}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})\oplus_{\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}\Big(\bigoplus\limits_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\mathrm{ST}^{\Delta}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\Big).

We have an inclusion of locally analytic
G
-representations
\pi^{-}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})\hookrightarrow\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
.

Corollary 6.13
.

[\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})]^{\Delta}
and the Hodge parameters of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}
are captured by
\pi^{-}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
and
\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
. In particular, if
\max_{1\leq i\leq s}l_{i}\leq 2
,
\pi^{-}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
determines
\mathbf{D}
.

Proof.

Write
\mathbf{D}=[\mathbf{D}_{0}-\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}]
, Theorem

6.10
shows that the Hodge parameters of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{cr}}
are captured by
\pi^{-}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
. On the other hand, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, the simple Hodge parameters in
p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref},u}([\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})])
are already encoded in
\mathrm{ST}^{\Delta}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
.
∎

6.3
Further comments

This section gives a possible picture to construct an explicit locally analytic representation
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{D})
that determines
\mathbf{D}
, which also gives another “evidence” (but still a conjecture) for the main theorems in Section
3.3
, and gives a complete version of the discussion in Section
6.2
. For any Steinberg
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{M}}
, assume that we have associated an explicit locally analytic representation
\pi_{1}({\mathbf{M}})
that determines
{\mathbf{M}}
(which are known for the case
\rk({\mathbf{M}})\leq 3
).

Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{\mathbf{P}_{S_{0}(u)}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi_{{\rm an}}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\eta_{S_{0}(u)}\right)^{{\rm an}},\;\pi_{{\rm an}}(\mathcal{F}_{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{s}\pi_{1}(E_{u,j}).\;

Let
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. By definition, we have
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{0}(u)}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0}(u)}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
. Put

\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\bigoplus^{\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}\limits_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\oplus_{Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)}\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}).

Note that
\pi^{\sharp}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{i}
. Suppose that
i
is the
j
-th simple root of
\Delta_{S_{0}(u),l}
for some
1\leq l\leq f_{u}
. Let
\pi_{i,u}
be the conjectural
j
-th branch of
\pi_{{\rm an}}(E_{u,i})
, see
[
7
, Conjecture 1.1]
. The locally analytic
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-conjecture predicates the following isomorphism of
\binom{r_{l,u}}{j}
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces (as in
[
5
, (43)]
):

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i,u}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}(\pi_{i,u},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!r_{l,u}-j}\!D_{{\rm dR}}(E_{u,l})\Big)\wedge\Big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i-(r_{l,u}-j)}\!D_{{\rm dR}}(E^{(l)}_{u})\Big)\subseteq\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D.

(6.22)

where
E^{(l)}_{u}:=[E_{u,l+1}-E_{u,l+2}-\cdots-E_{u,f_{u}}]
is the unique quotient of
\mathbf{D}
. Note that
\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i-(r_{l,u}-j)}\!D_{{\rm dR}}(E^{(l)}_{u})
is already an
E
-line. Then for each
i\in\Delta
, we thus get a surjection:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i}:=\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{i}}\epsilon_{i,u}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{i}}\pi_{i,u},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\rightarrow\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D.

(6.23)

Finally, we can define a surjection of finite dimensional
E
-vector spaces which only depends on the isomorphisms
(\epsilon_{i,u})_{i\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}^{\flat}_{i}}
and on a choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
:

t^{+}_{D}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\oplus{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

Such map is also suggested by
[
5
, (6)]
for general de Rham
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{D}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
. Moreover, we can further extend (
6.21
) to

\displaystyle\gamma^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{S_{0}(u)}}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{S_{0}(u)}}}\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\prod_{i=1}^{f_{u}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}\big(E_{u,i},E_{u,i}\big)

(6.24)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\prod_{i=1}^{f_{u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{r_{u,i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\big(\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi}_{E_{u,i}},{\mathbf{h}}_{E_{u,i}}),\pi_{{\rm an}}(E_{u,i})\big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

We expect:

Conjecture 6.14
.

Let
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
. Let
\iota_{S_{0}(u)}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\hookrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
be the natural inclusion. Recall the surjection
g^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
in Corollary
4.13
.

(1)

Under the splitting in Proposition
4.8
,
t^{+}_{D}\circ\gamma^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}|_{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{S_{0}(u)}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})}
is equal to
f_{\mathbf{D}}\circ\iota_{S_{0}(u)}
. Moreover,
g^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}
factors through
\gamma_{\mathbf{D}}
, and
g^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}=t^{+}_{D}\circ\gamma^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}
.

(2)

\ker(t^{+}_{D})
determines
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{\bullet}(D)
.

We end this section with further properties of
\pi^{\Diamond}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
and obtain a map

t^{\Diamond}_{D}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\Diamond}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\Diamond}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D),

for some
{\rm Hom}^{\Diamond}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)

Let
\pi^{\infty}_{\emptyset,i,u}
be the unique generic irreducible constituent of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{i,u}(\underline{\phi})
. Recall the locally analytic representations in (
8.3
):

\pi_{\min}^{-}(\underline{\phi}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}},{\mathbf{h}}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{\min}(\underline{\phi}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}},{\mathbf{h}}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}}).

For
\ast\in\{-,\emptyset\}
, let
\pi^{\ast}_{i,u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=\Big(\pi^{\infty}_{\emptyset,i,u}\otimes_{E}L_{i}(\lambda)\Big)\times_{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}},{\mathbf{h}}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}})}\pi^{\ast}_{\min}(\underline{\phi}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}},{\mathbf{h}}_{R_{u,i}^{i+1}})
. Put

R^{\ast}_{i,u}:=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi^{\ast}_{i,u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\eta_{i}\right)^{{\rm an}}.\;

(6.25)

We have the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow R^{-}_{i,u}\rightarrow R_{i,u}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\Big(\overline{M}(-s_{i}\cdot\lambda),{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{s_{i}u})\eta\Big)\rightarrow 0.

Note that

\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{\widehat{i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\Big(\overline{L}(-s_{i}\cdot\lambda),\big({\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cap{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{s_{i}u})\eta\big)^{\infty}\Big)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\Big(\overline{M}(-s_{i}\cdot\lambda),{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{s_{i}u})\eta\Big).

and
C_{i,[s_{i}u]_{i}}
appears as an irreducible consisitent of the first term. Let
\mathrm{ST}^{\ast}_{i,u}
be the unique quotient of
R^{\ast}_{i,u}
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. On the other hand, by Schraen’s spectral sequence (see
[
29
, Corollary 4.5]
), there is a bijection

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),R_{i,u}\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\left({\mathrm{H}}_{0}\big(\overline{\mathbf{N}}_{i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\big),\pi_{i,u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

(6.26)

In particular, we get an inclusion
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\left(\pi^{\infty}_{i,u}\otimes_{E}L_{i}(\lambda),\pi_{i,u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),R_{i,u}\right)
.By composing with the push-forward map via the map

, we actually obtain a map

\zeta_{i,u}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\left(\pi^{\infty}_{i,u}\otimes_{E}L_{i}(\lambda),\pi_{i,u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\Diamond}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

Similar to
[
4
]
, for any
u\in\mathcal{I}^{\flat}_{i}
, the following locally analytic representation

(the so-called extension square of locally analytic representations) should exists in

or
\mathrm{ST}_{i,u}/(\mathrm{ST}_{i,u})^{{\rm lalg}}
:

\textstyle{C_{s_{i-1}s_{i},\ast}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{i,u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(I_{u}^{-},I_{[s_{i}u]_{i}}^{+},\lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{i,[s_{i}u]_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{s_{i+1}s_{i},\ast^{\prime}}}

(6.27)

where
C_{s_{i-1}s_{i},\ast}=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{\widehat{i-1}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\Big(\overline{L}(-s_{i-1}s_{i}\cdot\underline{\lambda}),\ast\Big)
(resp.,
C_{s_{i+1}s_{i},\ast^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{P}}}_{\widehat{i+1}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\Big(\overline{L}(-s_{i+1}s_{i}\cdot\underline{\lambda}),\ast^{\prime}\Big)
) for some irreducible non-generic smooth representation
\ast
(resp.,
\ast^{\prime}
) of
\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i-1}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
(resp.,
\mathbf{L}_{\widehat{i+1}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
). We see that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(C^{\Diamond}_{i,u},\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\big)=2
.

Fix
V^{\Diamond}_{i,u}
a locally analytic representation of
G
, which is isomorphic to a non-split extension of
C^{\Diamond}_{i,u}
by
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
, i.e.,

V^{\Diamond}_{i,u}:=[\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})-C^{\Diamond}_{i,u}],

By (
6.5
),

. Note that

, and thus fix an injection

. We have

. Moreover, the unique non-split extension in

gives a non-split extension of
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
by

, which has the following form:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern (6.28)

By Lemma

, we see that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),V^{\Diamond}_{i,u}\right)=n+2
. For any

, we put
I_{i}(u)_{[s_{i}]}^{c}:=\big(I_{i}(u)^{c}\backslash\{u^{-1}(j+1)\}\big)\cup\{u^{-1}(j)\}
and fix the following
isomorphisms of

-dimensional
