-vector spaces:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i,[s_{i}u]_{i}}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}(C_{i,[s_{i}u]_{i}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}))\xrightarrow{\sim}Ee_{I_{i}(u)_{[s_{i}]}^{c}}\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D,

(6.29)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\Diamond}_{i,u}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}(C^{\Diamond}_{i,u},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}))\xrightarrow{\sim}Ee_{I_{i}(u)^{c}}\oplus Ee_{I_{i}(u)_{[s_{i}]}^{c}}\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D,

Then we get for each
i\in\Delta
a map:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\Diamond}_{i}:=\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}^{\flat}_{i}}\epsilon^{\Diamond}_{i,u}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}^{\flat}_{i}}C^{\Diamond}_{i,u},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\rightarrow\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D.

(6.30)

Let
\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D\big)^{\Diamond}
be the image of
\epsilon^{\Diamond}_{i}
and let
\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D\big)^{\Diamond,c}
be its complement in
\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}D
. Let
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D)^{\Diamond}
be the image of
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D)\hookrightarrow\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}D\twoheadrightarrow\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}D/\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D\big)^{\Diamond,c}\cong\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D\big)^{\Diamond}
. Denote

{\rm Hom}^{\Diamond}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D):=\sum_{i\in\Delta}g_{i}\Big({\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W\big)^{\Diamond},{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D)^{\Diamond}\Big)\Big)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

(6.31)

Therefore, we obtain a surjection of finite dimensional
E
-vector spaces which only depends on the
(\epsilon^{\Diamond}_{u,i})_{i\in\Delta,u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and on a choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
:

t^{\Diamond}_{D}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\Diamond}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\Diamond}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

On the other hand, we have a natural map (recall the definition of
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u,[i]}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
after (
4.1
)):

\displaystyle\gamma^{\Diamond}_{\mathbf{D}}:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}\\
i\in\Delta\end{subarray}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u,[i]}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}\\
i\in S_{0}(u)\end{subarray}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(R_{u,i}^{i+1},R_{u,i}^{i+1})\times\prod_{j\neq i,i+1}{\rm Ext}^{1}(R_{u,j},R_{u,j})

(6.32)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}\\
i\in S_{0}(u)\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathbf{L}_{i}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\left(\pi^{\infty}_{i,u}\otimes_{E}L_{i}(\lambda),\pi_{i,u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{u,i}\zeta_{i,u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{\Diamond}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

Let
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
and
i\in\Delta
. Let
\iota_{u,[i]}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u,[i]}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\hookrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
be the natural inclusion. It should be true that:

(1)

{\rm Hom}^{\Diamond}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)\cong\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})^{\Delta}_{/S_{0}}
(see (
4.23
)).

(2)

Under the splitting in Proposition
4.8
,
t^{\Diamond}_{D}\circ\gamma^{\Diamond}_{\mathbf{D}}|_{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u,[i]}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})}
is equal to
f_{\mathbf{D}}\circ\iota_{u,[i]}
. Moreover,
g^{\Diamond}_{\mathbf{D}}
factors through
\gamma^{\Diamond}_{\mathbf{D}}
, and
g^{\Diamond}_{\mathbf{D}}=t^{\Diamond}_{D}\circ\gamma^{\Diamond}_{\mathbf{D}}
.

(3)

For
i\in\Delta
, define
D^{\Diamond,(i)}:=E\langle e_{t}:t\notin I^{c}_{u,i}\cup\{u^{-1}(i)\},\forall\;u\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{i}\rangle
and
D^{\Diamond}_{(i)}=E\langle e_{t}:t\in I^{c}_{u,i}\cup\{u^{-1}(i)\}\text{\;for some\;}u\in\mathcal{I}^{\sharp}_{i}\rangle\subseteq D
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\Diamond}_{i}
be the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D/D^{\Diamond,(i)}=D^{\Diamond}_{(i)}
. Then
\ker(t^{\Diamond}_{D})
determines
\{\mathcal{L}^{\Diamond}_{i}\}_{i\in\Delta}
when modulo the action of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}
.

For
?\in\{+,\Diamond\}
, let
\pi^{-,?}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
be the unique quotient of the tautological extension of
\ker(t^{?}_{D})\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. If
\max_{1\leq i\leq s}l_{i}\leq 2
,
\pi^{-,\Diamond}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
is equal to
\pi^{-,+}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
.

6.4
Universal extensions

Let
R_{\mathbf{D}}
be the universal deformation ring of deformations of
\mathbf{D}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, let
R_{\mathbf{D},u}
be the universal deformation ring of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
-trianguline deformations of
\mathbf{D}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
, and let
R_{\mathbf{D},g}
be the universal deformation ring of de Rham deformations. Let
I_{u}
(resp.,
I_{g}
) be the kernel of
R_{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow R_{\mathbf{D},u}
(resp.,
R_{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow R_{g}
). Then
R_{\mathbf{D},u,g}\cong R_{\mathbf{D}}/(I_{u}+I_{g})
is the universal deformation ring of de Rham
\mathcal{F}_{u}
-trianguline deformations of
\mathbf{D}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. We have natural surjections
R_{\mathbf{D}}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\mathbf{D},u}\rightarrow R_{\mathbf{D},u,g}
. For a continuous character
\delta
of
{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, denote by the
R_{\delta}\cong E[[U,T]]
(resp.
R_{\delta,g}
) the universal deformations ring (resp., the universal deformations ring of locally algebraic deformations) of
\delta
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. We have natural surjection
R_{\delta}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\delta,g}
. For a complete local Noetherian
E
-algebra
R
, we use
{\mathfrak{m}}_{R}
(or use
{\mathfrak{m}}
for simplicity when it does not cause confusion) to denote its maximal ideal. Then for
\ast\in\{\emptyset,g,u\}
, we have
({\mathfrak{m}}_{R_{\mathbf{D},\ast}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R_{\mathbf{D},\ast}})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\ast}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
. Moreover,
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{\delta})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
and
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{\delta,g})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
. Let
\mathcal{H}
be the Bernstein centre over
E
associated to
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{\phi})
, and let
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\underline{\phi}}
be the completion of
\mathcal{H}
at
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{\phi})
. We have isomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\underline{\phi}}\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}},g}
by sending a smooth deformation
\chi
of
{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}}
to
\big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\eta\chi z^{-{\mathbf{h}}}\big)^{\infty}
. Moreover, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, we have the following commutative Cartesian diagram over
{\rm Art}_{E}
:

\textstyle{R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{u}}
\textstyle{R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}},g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{u}}
\textstyle{R_{\mathbf{D},u}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{\mathbf{D},u,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2},}

(6.33)

Let
A_{\mathbf{D}}:=R_{\mathbf{D}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\times_{R_{\mathbf{D},g}}A_{0}
with
A_{0}:=R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi})z^{{\mathbf{h}}},g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\hookrightarrow R_{\mathbf{D},g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
, and
A_{\mathbf{D},u}:=R_{\mathbf{D},u}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\times_{R_{\mathbf{D},u,g}}A_{0,u}
with
A_{0,u}:=R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}},g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\hookrightarrow R_{\mathbf{D},u,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
. The tangent space of
A_{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
A_{\mathbf{D},u}
) is naturally isomorphism to
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})
(resp.,
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\cong{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
). We denote the kernel of
A_{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow A_{u}
with
\mathcal{I}_{u}
. Let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{D}}
be the kernel of the natural morphism
A_{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}}A_{u}
. Note that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{D}}=\mathcal{I}_{1}
(resp.,
I_{\mathbf{D}}=0
) if
\mathbf{D}
is Steinberg (resp., crystalline). Put
A_{\mathbf{D},S_{0}}:=A_{\mathbf{D}}/\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{D}}
.

Similarity, let
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
(resp.,
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
) be the universal extension of
\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{\mathbf{D}})\otimes\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
(resp.,
\zeta_{u}({\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E))\otimes\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
) by
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
(see (
6.21
) for the map
\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{\mathbf{D}})
). Morevoer, let
\delta_{u}:={\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\eta\chi_{\lambda}
and let
\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
be the universal extension of
{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\otimes_{E}\delta_{u}
by
\delta_{u}
, so that
the induced representation
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
is the universal extension of
\zeta_{u}({\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E))\otimes\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
. Composed the above universal extensions with the pull-back map for the natural map

p_{u}:\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{u}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
, we get a locally analytic representation
(I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-})^{\flat}
which is the universal extension of
\zeta_{u}({\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E))\otimes\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
.

Let
U_{u}
be the intersection of
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ}}
with the kernel of the projection
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
, then we have hence an isomorphisms of
G
-representations

\displaystyle\left((I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-})^{\flat}/U_{u}\right)\oplus_{\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})}\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}.

(6.34)

We have a natural action of
A_{\mathbf{D},u}\hookrightarrow R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
on
\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
where
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{R_{{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})z^{{\mathbf{h}}}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\cong{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)^{\vee}
acts via
x:\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\otimes_{E}\delta_{u}\xrightarrow{x}\delta_{u}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
. Similarity,
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
admits an
A_{u}
-action, which is given by

x:\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\rightarrow\zeta_{u}({\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E))\otimes\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\xrightarrow{x}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-},

where
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{u}}\cong{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\zeta_{u}}(\zeta_{u}({\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E))^{\vee}
. Moreover,
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}/U_{u}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
is
A_{u}
-equivalent.

Proposition 6.15
.

There is a unique
A_{\mathbf{D}}
-action on
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
such that for any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
, we have an
A_{\mathbf{D},u}\times G
-equivariant injection
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}[\mathcal{I}_{u}]
.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.12
, we define an
A_{\mathbf{D}}
-action on
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
by letting
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{\mathbf{D}}}\cong\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(\mathbf{D},\mathbf{D})\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{\mathbf{D}})^{\vee}
act via
x:\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{\mathbf{D}})^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
. This action satisfies the property in the theorem. The uniqueness follows from the fact that
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
is generated by
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
.
∎

7
Local-global compatibility

7.1
Patched eigenvariety and Hecke eigenvariety

We follow the notation of
[
16
, Section 4.1.1]
and
[
13
, Section 2]
, and recall briefly the patched eigenvariety.

We have a patched Galois deformation ring
R_{\infty}=R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}
, where
{\mathfrak{p}}
(denoted
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}
in
loc. cit.
) is a
p
-adic place of a totally real subfield
F^{+}
, and
\overline{r}:{\rm Gal}_{F^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
is a continuous representation admitting a
suitable globalization

\overline{\rho}
(see
[
13
, Section 2.1]
). Here
R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}
denotes the maximal reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of the universal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lifting ring of
\overline{r}
. We have an
R_{\infty}
-admissible unitary representation
\Pi_{\infty}
of
G
over
E
(i.e., the so-called patched Banach representation). We refer to
loc. cit.
for details. Let
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}
be the
G
-subrepresentation of locally
R_{\infty}
-analytic vectors in
\Pi_{\infty}
(see
[
9
, Section 3.1]
). In this paper, we assume that
F^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\cong{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
.

Put
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}:=(\Spf R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}=({\rm Spf}\;R_{\overline{r}}^{\square})^{{\rm rig}}
, so that

{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}:=(\Spf R_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
. By
[
16
, Section 4.1.2]
, we see that
J_{\mathbf{B}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}
is a coadmissible module over
\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{T})
, which corresponds to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
over
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{T}
such that

\Gamma\Big({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{T},\mathcal{M}_{\infty}\Big)\cong J_{\mathbf{B}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}.

The patched eigenvariety
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{T}
is defined to be the Zariski-closed support of
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
. By
[
16
, Theorem 4.1]
, the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
is Cohen-Macaulay over
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. Moreover, for
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\chi_{x})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{T}
,
x\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
if and only if

J_{\mathbf{B}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y},{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})=\chi_{x}]\neq 0
.

The patched eigenvariety
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
are related to the trianguline variety
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
in
[
9
]
. Let
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
be the automorphism defined by

\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\delta_{1},\cdots,\delta_{n}):=\delta_{\mathbf{B}}\cdot(\delta_{1},\delta_{2}{\rm unr}(p^{-1}),\cdots,\delta_{n}{\rm unr}(p^{-(n-1)}))=(\delta_{1},\cdots,\delta_{n})\cdot\zeta,

where
\zeta:=\big({\rm unr}(p^{1-n}),\cdots,{\rm unr}(p^{i-n})z^{i-1},\cdots,\;z^{n-1}\big)
. Then
\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
induces an isomorphism of rigid spaces
\mathrm{id}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}:\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\mathcal{T}
. Let
\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big)
be the image of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
via this automorphism. Then the natural embedding
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times\mathcal{T}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times\mathcal{T}

factors through

X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})\big).

(7.1)

so that
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}
induces morphisms
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}:X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
.

7.2
Main theorem

This section follows the route of
[
15
, Section 4.1]
. We fix a Galois representation
\rho\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}
. We make the following Hypothesis.

Hypothesis 7.1
.

(Keep the notation in Section
2.2
)

(a)

\rho_{p}:=\rho|_{{\rm Gal}_{F^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
is a generic semistable
p
-adic Galois representation with Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}
.

(b)

Let
\mathbf{D}:=D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{p})
. For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
,
\mathbf{D}
admits a non-critical triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{u}
with parameters
\delta_{\underline{\phi},u}
.

(c)

Put
\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}:={\mathbf{h}}-\theta\in X_{\Delta}^{+}
and
y_{u}:=(\rho,\delta_{\mathbf{B}}{\rm unr}(\underline{\phi}^{u})\chi_{{\lambda_{{\mathbf{h}}}}})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho},U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}\times\mathcal{T}
, then
y_{u}\in X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
, where
\delta_{\mathbf{B}}=\eta^{-2}
is the modulus character of
\mathbf{B}
.

Note that
x_{u}:=\iota^{-1}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(y_{u})=(\rho_{p},\delta_{\underline{\phi},u})\in X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
. Similar to
[
16
, Proposition 4.3, Corollary 4.4 and Lemma 4.6]
, the non-critical assumption and
[
13
, Theorem 4.35]
show that

Lemma 7.2
.

(1)

x_{u}
is a smooth point of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
and
y_{u}
is a non-critical classical smooth point of
X_{{\mathfrak{p}}}(\overline{\rho})
. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
is locally free of rank
1
at
y_{u}
.

(2)

y_{u}
does not admit companion points of non-dominant weight.

Recall that the completion of
R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}[1/p]
at
\rho_{p}
is natural to
R^{\Box}_{\rho_{p}}\cong R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}
, where
R^{\Box}_{\rho_{p}}
is the framed universal deformation ring of
\rho
(over
{\rm Art}_{E}
). Let
R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}:=R_{\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{R_{\rho_{p}}}R^{\Box}_{\rho_{p}}
. Put
R_{\mathbf{D},u}^{\Box}:=R_{\mathbf{D}}^{\Box}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}R_{\mathbf{D},u}
for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
. As
x_{u}
in non-critical, the completion of
X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r})
at
x_{u}
is naturally isomorphic to
R_{\mathbf{D},u}^{\Box}
. Keep the notation in Section
6.4
and the discussion below
[
15
, (4.3)]
.

Let
{\mathfrak{a}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}}
be an ideal of
R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}
satisfies that
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{\mathbf{D}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}}
. Then the composition
A_{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D}}/{\mathfrak{a}}
is an isomorphism (i.e.,
{\mathfrak{a}}
delete the information comes from framing). In particular, we get
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{\mathbf{D},u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{\mathbf{D},u}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u,g}}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{\mathbf{D},g}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{\mathbf{D},g}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},g}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},g}}
, so that

A_{\mathbf{D},u}\hookrightarrow R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}/{\mathfrak{a}}
and
A_{\mathbf{D},u,g}\hookrightarrow R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},g}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},g}/{\mathfrak{a}}
are isomorphisms too. We use
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}[1/p]
denote the preimage of
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}:=({\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho},{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})\subseteq{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}:=({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})\subseteq R_{\infty}[1/p]
.

By
[
15
, Lemma 4.2]
, we have

\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]=\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{\mathbf{D}}}]
. By replacing the reference
[
13
]
in the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 4.2 (2)]
with
[
26
, Lemma 5.2, Theorem 5.6 and Proposition 5.4]
, we also get that

{\rm Hom}_{G}(\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}\big(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]\big)\cong{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]\big)\cong E.

Let
U_{u}=U_{{\mathfrak{p}},u}\times U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{u}\subseteq\iota_{{\mathfrak{p}}}\big(X_{\mathrm{tri}}(\overline{r}))\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
be a smooth affinoid neighourhood of
y_{u}
such
y_{u^{\prime}}\not\in U_{{\mathfrak{p}},u}
for
u^{\prime}\neq u
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}}
be the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}(U_{{\mathfrak{p}},w})
at
y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}\supset{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}}
be the closed ideal generated by the above ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}[1/p]
. Consider
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}:=\mathcal{M}_{\infty}/({\mathfrak{a}}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})
. There are natural
{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\times R_{\infty}
-equivariant injections:

{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow J_{\mathbf{B}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}].

Since
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
is locally free of rank
1
at all
y_{u}
, we obtain that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\cong R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}/{\mathfrak{a}}
, so that
\dim_{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}=1+d_{u}
, where
d_{u}:=\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E))
. In this case, we have a
{\mathbf{T}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\times A_{\mathbf{D},u}
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}_{y_{u}}\cong\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\delta_{\mathbf{B}}
. Similar to the proof of
[
16
, Lemma 4.11]
, the maps
{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]
are balanced by using the non-critical assumption), hence induces a
G\times R_{\infty}
-equivariant injection:

\displaystyle\iota_{u}:I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}].\;

(7.2)

Recall that
U_{u}
is the intersection of
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
with
\ker(\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}))
. Similar to the argument in the proof of
[
16
, Proposition 4.7 (a)]
, we see that
\iota_{u}
factors through the projection
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\twoheadrightarrow I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}/U_{u}
. Similar to
[
15
, Lemma 4.4]
, we have (recall the map
p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref},u}
in (
3.25
))

Proposition 7.3
.

We have
\mathrm{ST}^{\Delta}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{u})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
. In particular,
\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{u})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
determines
[\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})]^{\Delta}=p^{\Delta}_{\mathrm{ref},u}([\underline{\mathscr{L}}(\mathbf{D})])
.

Proof.

The argument in the proof of
[
15
, Lemma 4.4]
show that
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{Im}\iota_{u})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
. We further show that
\mathrm{ST}^{\Delta}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{Im}\iota_{u})[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
, this assertion is essentially obtained by applying the proof in
[
16
, Theorem 4.10]
to the Steinberg blocks
\{E_{u,i}\}_{1\leq i\leq f_{u}}
of the
\mathbf{P}_{S_{0}(u)}
-parabolic filtration
\mathcal{F}_{S_{0}(u)}
. We see that the representation
\mathrm{ST}^{\Delta}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})
lives in
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
, by the argument around
[
16
, (96)]
(and a similar strategy as in the first paragraph of
[
15
, Lemma 4.4]
), such representation is also killed by
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
. We complete the proof.
∎

Let
\widetilde{\pi}
be the subrepresentation of
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]
generated by
\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{u})
for all
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{S_{0}}
. Note that
\widetilde{\pi}
has an
A_{\mathbf{D}}
-action via
A_{\mathbf{D}}\xrightarrow{\sim}R^{\Box}_{\mathbf{D},u}/{\mathfrak{a}}\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{\infty}[1/p]/{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}
.

Theorem 7.4
.

There is an
A_{\mathbf{D}}\times G
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{\pi}\cong\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
. Moreover,
\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\pi}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
.

Proof.

Similar to the argument in the proof
[
15
, Theorem 4.5]
, we see that
\widetilde{\pi}
is a subrepresentation of
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ}}
and contains all
I^{G}_{{\overline{\mathbf{B}}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
so that
\widetilde{\pi}\cong\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
. We equip
\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
with an
A_{\mathbf{D}}
-action by the
A_{\mathbf{D}}
-action on
\widetilde{\pi}
(induced from
R_{\infty}
) and get a
G\times A_{\mathbf{D}}
-isomorphism
\widetilde{\pi}\cong\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
. We thus see that
\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})=\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{\mathbf{D}}}]\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\pi}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
. The last assertion follows.
∎

In particular, by Corollary
6.13
, we obtain:

Corollary 7.5
.

Keep the situation.
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
determines
\mathbf{D}
if
\max_{1\leq l\leq s}l_{i}\leq 2
.

8
Appendix: explicit examples for
{\rm GL}_{2}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
and
{\rm GL}_{3}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})

In this section, we give some explicit examples of locally analytic representations
\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
that determine
\mathbf{D}
. Such representations
\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
are expected to be locally analytic subrepresentations of the conjectural locally analytic representation
\pi^{{\rm an}}(\rho_{p})
via the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence.

8.1
{\rm GL}_{2}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})

Suppose that
\underline{\phi}=(\phi,\phi p)
for some
\phi\in E^{\times}
. Let
\mathbf{D}=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi)z^{h_{1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi p)z^{h_{2}})]
(a non-split extension) be a non-critical semistable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module. Recall the following perfect cup product (see the argument around
[
16
, (61)]
):

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi p)z^{h_{2}}),\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi)z^{h_{1}}))\times

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{1})z^{h_{2}}),\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi)z^{h_{1}}))

(8.1)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cup}{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi p)z^{h_{2}}),\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi)z^{h_{1}}))\cong E

Note that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi)z^{h_{2}}),\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi)z^{h_{1}}))\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
, where the latter space is a
2
-dimensional
E
-linear space spanned by
\val_{p}
and
\log_{p}
. Let
\mathscr{L}(\mathbf{D})=(E[\mathbf{D}])^{\perp}
via such perfect pairing.

We have
\Delta=\{1\}
, so that
Q^{\Diamond}_{\{1\}}(\emptyset,\lambda)={\rm St}_{2}^{\infty}(\lambda)
and
Q^{\Diamond}_{\{1\}}(\{1\},\lambda)=L(\lambda)
. Put

\displaystyle I(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})=\mathcal{F}^{{\rm GL}_{2}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{\overline{B}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\left(L(-s_{1}\cdot\lambda),{\rm unr}(\phi)\otimes{\rm unr}(\phi)\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})=\mathcal{F}^{{\rm GL}_{2}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}_{\overline{B}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\left(L(-s_{1}\cdot\lambda),|\cdot|^{-1}{\rm unr}(\phi)\otimes|\cdot|{\rm unr}(\phi)\right).\;

Suppose that
\mathbf{D}
is crystalline. In this case,
\mathscr{L}(\mathbf{D})=E\val_{p}
and
\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
has the form

{\rm St}^{\infty}_{2}(\lambda)

L(\lambda)

I(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})

\widetilde{I}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})

.

\pi_{c}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}}):=I(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})-L(\lambda)-\widetilde{I}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})

\displaystyle\pi^{-}_{\min}(\mathbf{D}):=\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}(\mathbf{D})=[\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\psi,{\mathbf{h}})-\pi^{-}_{c}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})]\hookrightarrow\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})=[\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\psi,{\mathbf{h}})-\pi_{c}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})].

(8.3)

Moreover, the non-split extensions
[\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})-\pi_{c}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})]
or
[\pi_{1}(\underline{\phi},{\mathbf{h}})-L(\lambda)]
encode exactly the information of
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{D})
.

8.2
{\rm GL}_{3}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})

Suppose that
\mathbf{D}=\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{1})z^{h_{1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{2})z^{h_{2}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{3})z^{h_{3}})
with
\phi_{i}=\alpha p^{i-1}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
and some
\alpha\in E^{\times}
. Let
{\rm St}^{\infty}_{3}(\lambda)
,
v^{\infty}_{P_{1}}(\lambda)
,
v^{\infty}_{P_{2}}(\lambda)
and
L(\lambda)
be the four locally algebraic generalized Steinberg representations of
{\rm GL}_{3}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, and we refer to
[
27
, Introduction]
for the same notation of Orlik-Strauch locally analytic representations
C^{2}_{s_{2},1}
,
C_{s_{2},s_{2}}
,
C^{2}_{s_{2},s_{2}s_{1}}
and
C^{2}_{s_{1},1}
,
C_{s_{1},s_{1}}
,
C^{2}_{s_{1},s_{1}s_{2}}
.

\mathbf{D}
is Steinberg (i.e.,
S_{0}(\mathbf{D})=\{1,2\}

\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
is already discussed in
[
7
]
,
[
4
]
. If
\mathbf{D}

), then
\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
has the form (see
[
22
, Introduction]
):

C^{2}_{s_{2},1}

C_{s_{2},s_{2}}

C^{2}_{s_{1},s_{1}s_{2}}

v^{\infty}_{P_{2}}(\lambda)

v^{\infty}_{P_{1}}(\lambda)

{\rm St}^{\infty}_{3}(\lambda)

C^{2}_{s_{1},1}

C_{s_{1},s_{1}}

C^{2}_{s_{2},s_{2}s_{1}}

v^{\infty}_{P_{1}}(\lambda)

v^{\infty}_{P_{2}}(\lambda)

{\rm St}^{\infty}_{3}(\lambda)

.

(8.5)

In this case (recall (
3.1
)), the simple Hodge parameter
\mathcal{L}_{21}
is encoded in
{\mathbb{W}}(1)\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}(1)
, which can be seen in the first branch of
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{D})
. The higher parameter
\mathcal{L}_{02}
is encoded in
{\mathbb{W}}(s_{1}s_{2})\subseteq{\mathbb{E}}(s_{1}s_{2})
, which can be seen in the second branch of
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{D})
.
\pi_{\min}(\mathbf{D})
is obtained by considering several parabolic inductions.

We first use the triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{1}
. Put
D_{1}:=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{1})z^{h_{1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{2})z^{h_{2}})]\hookrightarrow\mathbf{D}
and
\mathbf{D}\twoheadrightarrow C_{1}^{\prime}:=[\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{2})z^{h_{2}})-\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(\phi_{3})z^{h_{3}})]
. By
[
4
, Remark (1)]
, we obtain two branches that correspond to the Steinberg
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
D_{1}
and the special crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
C_{1}^{\prime}
, i.e.,

C^{2}_{s_{2},1}

C_{s_{2},s_{2}}

v^{\infty}_{P_{2}}(\lambda)

{\rm St}^{\infty}_{3}(\lambda)

C^{2}_{s_{1},1}

C_{s_{1},s_{1}}

C^{1}_{s_{2}s_{1},1}
